This review finds eleven plausible additions through their winning entries,
one plausible note through an alternative Coffee entry, and eight weak or
unresolved winning connections. These are not twelve measured correct automatic
admissions or an accuracy estimate. In particular, the coffee note at rank 7
cannot validate the selected Time after 9 a.m. connection merely because another
entry provides a plausible route.

### How the large group scores arise

All three Children cases select the same heterogeneous three-entry G498 group:
two Dining experiences definitions and one Dining groups definition. The latter
reads, "Dining groups are parties of two or more people dining together, which
may include children." It contributes 93.05%, 87.70% and 91.28% of those cases'
winning group affinities. For L04215, its cosine is 0.55770, whereas the other
two members are -0.00017 and -0.09150. Thus taking a group mean does not ensure
agreement across its members: the transformed affinity is dominated by one
optional-mention match. This is a concrete mechanism in the winning group, not
an ablation proving which scoring change would resolve it.

The automotive Simple habits case is different. Its three Diet member cosines
are 0.54354–0.58168 and the strongest contributes only 37.74% of the group
affinity. Shared routine/pattern language is consistent across the group.
Removing single-member dominance alone would therefore not address all of the
observed weak connections.

These concerns do not rely on treating the root as Food only. They concern what
the actual member meanings substantively establish: dining together versus an
optional participant, or eating patterns versus a generic repeated behavior.
The user-rejected Easter broadcast remains the only definite rejected control
in this new ranking test; the additional concerns remain agent assessments.

### Existing baseline and controls

The declared full-member baseline puts the cannabis/Times of day note 77th
instead of 10th, automotive Simple habits 105th instead of 11th, the Anne Frank
Children note 76th instead of 13th, and Breath 165th instead of 16th. It retains
the Egg yolks note at rank 1, the two Bread notes at ranks 2 and 3, and Coffee
shops at rank 6. These are descriptive rank comparisons using each method's own
best entry per note. The baseline uses full members whereas the asymmetric
score uses grouped members; this does not isolate group maximization from the
reference-cohort change. The subsequent complete top-twenty review is recorded
below; neither these ranks alone nor that agent review establishes comparative
precision or general superiority.

The earlier human-accepted egg-decorating note ranks 29th under the asymmetric
method, still through L02984 with its unchanged score 6.1746. The rejected
broadcast retains 2.29054. Of the 281 omitted note maxima, 121 exceed that
broadcast score. This is not evidence that 121 notes should be admitted or a
chosen cutoff. The observed weak matches already interleave with useful
candidates well above it.

All eight G463 entries are retained as controls, producing sixteen exact-query
and source-note-exclusion score replays to less than `1e-12` absolute error.
For genuinely omitted notes, no reference entry shares their source note, so
the same-note exclusion control leaves every score unchanged. No external
model calls, new SSE encodings, training or hierarchy changes occur.

The numerical matrix is 1,431 queries including controls by 1,648 existing
reference entries, rather than an all-originals square matrix. Native request
texts, full-meaning spans and receipt row identities are checked for all 6,991
saved vectors. Independent scalar checks cover all 246,132 original-group mean
affinities, both scores for every entry, and both complete note rankings.
Sixteen frozen source hashes and all saved output hashes pass. HR35 remains
unchanged at 718 reachable notes.

All twenty are provisionally plausible note additions on this agent review.
Sixteen have straightforward source/member connections; four need explicit
qualifications. Knives includes uses beyond dining even though its post is
about eating burgers. Wagamama's post concerns a student DJ night rather than
food being served, but its selected topic is the identified restaurant chain.
Seasonings occurs in a legal/branding story. The seed-oil note supplies a
dietary-exposure connection, while its selected consumer-body meaning is broader
than the physiological effects discussed. This review classifies the topic; it
does not establish the source's biological claim. These are four qualified
plausible notes, not four definite rejections or silent human approvals.

Actual member links include Changes in tea culture to Food `L01112`, whose
meaning includes cuisine traditions (cosine 0.73811), and Mugs to Dishes `L00170`,
whose definition includes kitchen/tableware cups (0.62304). Steaks matches
prepared/ingredient Food definitions, including L00124 at 0.60151. These are
specific member connections, without using a synthetic root vector as the
admission target. The mean score itself uses every member, not only these
displayed nearest matches.

### Matched comparison and limits

The two twenty-note selections share eleven notes. The averaging method brings
in nine different notes: Changes in tea culture, Wagamama, Coffee beans, the
seed-oil consumer's body, Mugs, Steaks, Tea, Coffee availability and Ice cream
bars. It leaves out all eight previously flagged weak/unresolved connections
from the asymmetric top twenty. It also leaves out the otherwise plausible
desk/coffee note whose asymmetric winner was Time after 9 a.m.; that note is
24th under averaging. Do not count that removal as a quality improvement.

Several shared notes use different winning entries. Averaging selects Knives
instead of Advice in the burger note, Chili dishes instead of Spiciness,
Milk instead of Praise for food and drink, Coffee roasting instead of Sipping,
and Tea instead of Drinking. Thus the comparison concerns both which notes are
retrieved and which original entries supply their proposed routes.

This larger source review supports retaining full-member averaging as the
stronger Food-collection ranking candidate for further investigation. It is not
20/20 measured accuracy, proof of whole-meaning containment, an admission
threshold or evidence that averaging works equally well for every root. Only
the pre-existing Egg yolks decision is human-labeled within these twenty; the
other assessments and qualifications remain the agent's. The root references
also contain repeated general Food definitions, so this result must not be
silently generalized to a root with a different topic mixture.

All twenty original post texts, complete winning meanings, unchanged scores and
ranking identities pass source checks. Thirteen protected source hashes and all
four review output hashes pass. Per-group affinity contributions plus the
originally ungrouped-member contribution sum to one for every reviewed case.
No new scores, encodings, model calls, training or hierarchy changes occur;
HR35 still reaches 718 notes. There is no applied note gain.

## Full-member averaging transferred to Tools and techniques

This check keeps the preceding Food method unchanged and uses the same 1,426
entries from 281 globally omitted notes. One additional omitted note has no
eligible saved vector and remains unscorable. The target reference is all 91
original members actually belonging to Tools and techniques `C00005`: 67 in its
fourteen original groups and 24 admitted originally ungrouped entries, covering
63 distinct notes. Earlier creation-only Tools probes used 67 references; their
scores must not be mistaken for this full-member transfer.

For each candidate original entry, transform every full-meaning cosine into
`exp[-5.5 × (1 − cosine)]`. Divide its mean over the 91 Tools members by its mean
over all 1,557 members of the other roots. Rank each omitted note by its
highest-scoring entry. No representation, beta, aggregation or threshold was
tuned for Tools. All reference comparisons reuse the saved cosine matrix.

The target is its actual collection of member meanings, rather than the
synthetic root definition. Another-root connection is not negative evidence;
multiple useful routes are allowed. A plausible note addition does not certify
containment of every possible use of its winning meaning.

## Investigating where admission should stop

The user asked for an ending method after the top-twenty reviews. This bounded
check keeps the full-member score unchanged. Before further source review it
freezes ranks 21–60 and ranks 80, 100, 120, 140, 160, 180, 200, 220, 240, 260 and
281 in each of Food `C00004` and Tools `C00005`. These 102 additional root/note
cases, plus the earlier forty, give 142 assessments: all first sixty results for
each root and eleven lower positions each. A note can appear under both roots;
142 is a count of root/note cases, not distinct notes or independent samples.

Each new case includes its full source post, winning meaning, five closest
actual members and the note's other eligible entries. The latter matter: PWM
wins a Food note whose post explicitly requests beer and has a Beer entry;
Solo entrepreneurship wins a Tools note explicitly discussing AI. Neither is
rejected solely because its winning heading is general. Source-dependent media
was not inspected. The root's synthetic definition remains outside the admission
target, and membership elsewhere remains compatible with a useful second route.

Agent assessments distinguish straightforward plausible, qualified plausible,
unresolved and not supported by the source/member evidence. The last category
does not become human gold. Exact earlier human note decisions remain separate.

| Reference collection | Straightforward plausible | Qualified plausible | Unresolved | Agent-assessed unsupported |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Food | 21 | 9 | 12 | 29 |
| Tools | 38 | 15 | 6 | 12 |

These deliberately selected ranks do not estimate overall precision or recall.
The score is a ratio of average affinities, not an admission probability. For
example, `score >= 1` selects 173 Food notes and 254 Tools notes from the 281
omitted notes; it does not imply that those selections are semantically suitable.

### Numerical ending rules

Three numerical ideas are checked without selecting their settings from the
semantic reviews: relative parity at one, a strongest score-curve bend in raw
or log scores, and the largest adjacent log-score drop. For the bend, normalize
rank and the descending score to `[0,1]`; take the largest positive difference
between the endpoint line `1 − normalized_rank` and the normalized score. The
selected rank is retained. This is a score-curve diagnostic, not HiGen's
HDBSCAN tree-cut algorithm. The largest-gap boundary lies between its two scores.

| Numerical rule | Food notes selected | Tools notes selected | Source-review result |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Ratio at least one | 173 | 254 | Contains at least 25 Food and 12 Tools assessed unsupported cases; most selections remain unreviewed. |
| Raw-score bend | 43 | 44 | All selections reviewed: six Food and two Tools assessed unsupported, plus seven and two unresolved cases respectively. |
| Log-score bend | 51 | 44 | Thirteen Food and two Tools assessed unsupported, plus eight and two unresolved cases respectively. |
| Largest adjacent log-score gap | 1 | 280 | Food stops after Egg yolks; Tools includes nearly everything, including twelve reviewed unsupported cases. |

Thus the tested numerical endpoints do not identify a useful semantic boundary.
Unlabeled tail positions cannot
be treated as negatives, and a score-distribution gap is not itself evidence of
irrelevance. The nonrandom review positions also do not support a confidence
claim about the semantic error rate on an unseen population.

### The specific orderings that obstruct a clean cutoff

| Collection | Higher-scoring, agent-assessed unsupported case | Lower-scoring useful case |
|---|---|---|
| Food | Rank 27: Wildlife `L04627`, score 2.7765; marked wild fish under investigation, with no established food role. | Rank 30: Traditional activities `L02986`, score 2.4377; the exact pisanki egg-decoration note previously accepted by the user. |
| Tools | Rank 28: Performance struggles `L02909`, score 3.3126; NBA team/player struggles, without an actual test or technical role. | Rank 31: Smartphones `L02428`, score 3.1720; satellite-texting functionality. |

The wildlife result's closest Food member is Food `L02407`, cosine 0.66572;
that member explicitly describes edible material. Wildlife similarity does not
establish that role in the candidate source. The NBA result's closest member
is Tests `L04780`, cosine 0.66798; expected sporting struggles do not establish
an actual evaluative check. These rejection assessments can be disputed, but
they are explicit source-based judgments rather than fabricated human labels.
If either judgment is upheld, a scalar threshold that retains the lower useful
case also retains the higher unsupported case.

This does **not** prove that no conservative scalar cutoff can be useful. The
user does not require every fitting note to be recovered. It establishes the
cost of treating a single score boundary as a complete separator.

### Retrospective conservative boundaries

For an explicit conservative alternative, take the prefix before the first
assessed unsupported **or unresolved** result, while still allowing qualified
plausible cases. The first blocking result is Food's Wildlife at rank 27 and
Tools' sparse Action: Up at rank 14. A midpoint between the last retained and
first blocked scores gives the following retrospective candidates:

| Collection | Candidate threshold | Omitted notes retained | Qualified cases retained |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Food | 2.814973 | 26 | 7 |
| Tools | 4.726646 | 13 | 1 |

These are 39 distinct omitted notes, not 39 applied additions. Their prefixes
have been source-reviewed, but the thresholds were chosen after those reviews.
They are not a separately validated stopping rule, and retaining qualified
cases is an explicit tradeoff. Food's boundary omits the human-approved
egg-decoration note and the straightforward coffee-flavor note at rank 33.
Tools' boundary omits Servers, Smartphones and many other software/device notes.
No acceptance percentage or error tolerance is invented on the user's behalf.

### Would those boundaries retain existing members?

A separate frozen follow-up checks original supporting notes already in the
reference collections. For each query, remove **all entries from its source
note** from both the target and other-root references. This prevents its own
meanings, including meanings in other roots, from supplying its support.
The planned calculation scores its original member entries. A supplemental
calculation allows every eligible original entry from that note, matching the
omitted-note maximum-entry policy and giving retention the stronger opportunity.

| Collection | Supporting notes checked | Retained using original member entries | Retained using any eligible entry in the note |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Food | 89 | 87 | 88 |
| Tools | 63 | 47 | 52 |

Food misses Charity dinners `L02693`, score 2.1061, even when considering all of
that note's entries. Its source explicitly describes attending a charity dinner
and paying for the table with bitcoin. Tools misses eleven notes under the
stronger all-entry calculation, including Software clients `L04264` at 2.2033,
Computers `L05212` at 4.0119 and Web development `L04757` at 4.4018. The software
client post explicitly discusses missing Primal support for NIP94 video events.
These are concrete useful routes lost by a high threshold, not merely abstract
low-score examples.

Not all historical memberships are established semantic positives: the missed
Tools cohort also includes political/military Experiments and music-proficiency
Tests. Its 52/63 retention is therefore a measured construction-membership result,
not validated recall. It also does not measure global note coverage: a note
lost from Tools may still have a useful route elsewhere.

### Result and retained evidence

No automatic ending method is qualified by these results. Raising a threshold
can deliberately recover fewer notes, but neither score parity nor an obvious
curve feature identifies the intended quality/omission tradeoff. The user then
authorized tracing the specific score inversions—Wildlife versus Charity dinners,
and NBA performance versus Software clients—and their components. That completed
diagnostic follows; it does not activate an admission rule.

## Tracing the score inversions by component

This follow-up freezes six exact queries before calculation: Wildlife `L04627`,
Charity dinners `L02693`, Traditional activities `L02986`, Performance struggles
`L02909`, Software clients `L04264` and Smartphones `L02428`. The egg-decoration
note's prior human acceptance remains note-level evidence; the new negative
assessments and other positive expectations remain the agent's. No meanings,
representations, reference memberships or beta settings change.

All comparisons remove every reference entry from the query's source note on
both sides. Wildlife, Traditional activities, Performance struggles and
Smartphones are globally omitted and therefore remove none. Charity dinners
removes four entries, leaving 174 Food references and 1,470 other-root references.
Software clients removes four, leaving 90 Tools references and 1,554 others.
Both existing-member scores reproduce the preceding whole-note-exclusion check.
This prevents their own entries or related same-post headings from providing
support. Original posts assess the examples; the saved SSE input remains title
plus full explicit meaning, without the post.

Let `A` be mean `exp[-5.5 × (1 − cosine)]` to actual target-root originals, and
`B` the pooled mean of the same affinities to actual originals in other roots.
The current diagnostic score is `A / B`. These are transformed cosine means,
not raw cosines or membership probabilities.

| Query | Target root | Within-root mean A | Other-root mean B | Ratio A/B |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| L04627 · Wildlife | Food | 0.015745 | 0.005671 | 2.7765 |
| L02693 · Charity dinners | Food | 0.023732 | 0.011268 | 2.1061 |
| L02986 · Traditional activities | Food | 0.019136 | 0.007850 | 2.4377 |
| L02909 · Performance struggles | Tools | 0.022287 | 0.006728 | 3.3126 |
| L04264 · Software clients | Tools | 0.051752 | 0.023489 | 2.2033 |
| L02428 · Smartphones | Tools | 0.040287 | 0.012701 | 3.1720 |

### The division reverses all four orderings

Within-root averaging already puts each useful case above its unsupported
counterpart. For an unsupported query `n` and useful query `p`, the exact
decomposition is:

`score(n) / score(p) = [A(n) / A(p)] × [B(p) / B(n)]`

| Compared entries: unsupported / useful | Target-mean factor | Other-root factor | Final ratio factor |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Wildlife / Charity dinners | 0.6634 | 1.9871 | 1.3183 |
| Wildlife / Traditional activities | 0.8228 | 1.3843 | 1.1390 |
| Performance struggles / Software clients | 0.4307 | 3.4912 | 1.5035 |
| Performance struggles / Smartphones | 0.5532 | 1.8878 | 1.0443 |

Every target-mean factor is below one: the useful query has stronger support.
Every other-root factor is large enough to reverse that ordering. Software
clients has about 2.32 times the within-Tools mean of Performance struggles,
but about 3.49 times its pooled other-root mean, leaving the NBA query ahead.

The other-root support is concrete. Software clients averages 0.027483 against
Information and 0.036544 against Finance/technology, versus 0.004521 and 0.004742
for Performance struggles. Charity dinners averages 0.016430 against
Finance/technology, compared with Wildlife's 0.005115. These are measured
root-collection means, not evidence that a particular word caused the embedding
behavior. Sharing a useful topic with another root does not invalidate the
target-root route; the division mixes strength of fit with distinctiveness.

### Averaging and closest matches have different effects

Wildlife's strongest individual Food match is Food `L02407`, cosine 0.66572,
above Charity dinners' strongest Food match at 0.60574. Thus a nearest-member
rule would still prefer Wildlife. Averaging all members already overcomes that
single misleading match in this example; the later division reintroduces the
wrong ordering.

For Performance struggles, the nine-member Tests/Testing/Experiments group
`G504` supplies 34.8% of its total Tools affinity, despite containing only 9.9%
of Tools references. Its group mean is 0.078309. Software clients' strongest
group, Data cables/Digital connections `G579`, averages 0.150609, and its
Software group `G058` averages 0.098505. Those useful subgroup links exist, but
the other-root division still reverses the best-group comparison for this pair.

The probe separates dropping originally ungrouped target members from changing
aggregation. It first compares the complete-member mean with the grouped-only
mean, keeping the denominator fixed. It then replaces that grouped mean with
the largest original-group mean, using exactly the same grouped references.
Originally ungrouped members are not fabricated into a semantic group.

### Broader matched check on the existing reviews

All 142 previous root/note cases retain their exact original winning entry and
meaning. No new labels or case winners are chosen for a method. Primary pair
counts compare straightforward plausible cases against agent-assessed unsupported
cases within each root; qualified and unresolved cases remain separate. Food
has 21 plausible and 29 unsupported cases, giving 609 dependent comparisons.
Tools has 38 and 12, giving 456. These totals are not admission accuracy or
independent validation of the earlier assessments.

| Score component choice | Food: useful above unsupported / 609 | Tools: useful above unsupported / 456 |
|---|---:|---:|
| All-member mean / other-root mean | 607 | 426 |
| All-member mean alone | 609 | 438 |
| Grouped-only mean / other-root mean | 607 | 415 |
| Best original-group mean / other-root mean | 530 | 349 |
| Best original-group mean alone | 531 | 389 |
| Nearest-member affinity / other-root mean | 543 | 359 |
| Nearest-member affinity alone | 544 | 405 |

Removing the denominator improves two Food comparisons without worsening any.
For Tools it improves 25 but worsens thirteen, a net gain of twelve. Those
thirteen are pair orderings, not thirteen distinct newly misclassified notes.
Dropping the 24 originally ungrouped Tools references worsens eleven comparisons.
Then changing the same grouped-only numerator to its best group improves four
and worsens seventy more. Thus stronger emphasis on one group is not supported
by these matched results.

The narrower ranks-21–60 cohort gives Food 98/100 before and 100/100 after
removing the division; Tools improves from 80/110 to 98/110, with 25 improvements
and seven regressions. Restricting to that cohort does not reverse the direction
of the finding, but neither cohort is a fresh random sample.

Eighteen Tools ordering failures remain with the within-root mean alone. They
involve six plausible entries and four assessed unsupported entries. Drones,
Factories, Cheap automotive fixes and T-Deck devices can score below Terminator
franchise, Staff appointments or generic Planning. These are remaining
disagreements involving the similarities, reference mix or provisional judgments;
the component probe does not settle their semantic labels. Removing the division
does not establish a clean boundary.

Food's 609/609 result excludes nine qualified and twelve unresolved cases. Its
lowest straightforward-plausible mean is Coffee flavour `L00683` at 0.017282,
just above the largest unsupported mean, Holidays `L05158` at 0.016672. Cost of
living, still unresolved, scores 0.038311. That unresolved overlap prevents
calling the observed plausible/unsupported gap a validated admission cutoff.

### Result, verification and next test

The arithmetic diagnosis is established for these fixed inputs: the other-root
division causes all four queried reversals. Removing it is the simpler candidate
supported by the matched checks. Best-group and nearest-member alternatives
perform worse here. The remaining regressions and uncertainty prevent adopting
it as an automatic admission or stopping rule yet.

The user then authorized the within-root mean across the complete omitted-note
ranking, including winner reselection, source review and boundary checks. That
completed test follows. The component diagnosis above deliberately keeps winners
fixed; its pair counts must not be used as quality measurements for the newly
selected entries. Old ratio thresholds are not reused on the new score scale.

Sixteen protected source hashes pass. The calculation checks 1,652 source-bound

reference scores reproduce, and all 28 panel-method count results pass. HR35's
frozen state is unchanged. No model calls, encodings, training, hierarchy changes
or applied note gains occur; the worktree remains for the saved evidence.

## Complete omitted-note ranking by within-root mean

This test uses all 1,426 eligible entries from the same 281 globally omitted
notes, separately against all 175 actual Food originals and all 91 actual Tools
originals. The additional omitted note without an eligible saved vector remains
unscorable. Each entry receives mean `exp[-5.5 × (1 − cosine)]` over that root's
members. A note receives its largest eligible entry score, with deterministic
entry-ID and note-ID ties. The full explicit-meaning vectors and their input
context containing the title and meaning remain unchanged; no synthetic root
vector is used.

All candidate notes are absent from the reference notes, so excluding the entire
query source note removes no references. Both 281-note rankings are rebuilt
from entry scores. Source hashes, review selection and candidate thresholds are
frozen before the new rankings or source assessments. No old ratio cutoff is
transferred to the new score scale.

### Notes and winning entries both change

| Root | Top-twenty notes shared with ratio ranking | Newly entering that top twenty | Top-twenty winning entries changed | Winning entries changed across all 281 notes |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Food | 16 | 4 | 6 | 174 |
| Tools | 13 | 7 | 2 | 100 |

For example, Food now selects Burgers `L00282` rather than general Knives in the
burger-eating note, Seed oils rather than the consumer's body in the seed-oil
note, and Bread preparation rather than Bread in the sourdough note. Tools
selects Automation rather than Work for the software-agent post and Gaming
rather than Open-source software in the Linux GPU-shim post. These are existing
meanings from the same notes; no meaning or graph node is rewritten.

The complete note ranking therefore tests more than the preceding fixed-entry
component comparison. A clearer entry can remove a qualification on an earlier
route, while an unrelated abstraction can expose a new route failure. The
prior assessments remain preserved; new winning-entry reviews are separate.

### Frozen candidate boundaries

Two experimental boundaries are taken from the earlier fixed-entry component
panel, without using new rankings or labels. The first is the largest mean
among previously assessed unsupported cases. The stricter one also includes
previously unresolved cases. A note is selected only if its new maximum-entry
score is **strictly greater** than the frozen boundary. Qualified plausible
cases are not silently treated as either negatives or established positives.

| Root | Above prior unsupported-case maximum | Notes selected | Above prior unsupported-or-unresolved maximum | Notes selected |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Food | 0.0166722209 | 43 | 0.0383115192 | 10 |
| Tools | 0.0273195401 | 64 | 0.0388707244 | 34 |

The first Food boundary comes from Holidays `L05158`, and its stricter boundary
from Cost of living `L02266`. Tools uses Terminator franchise `L04683` and then
Actions `L01600`. These are retrospective agent-reviewed controls used for a
frozen transfer check, not human-certified probabilities or guaranteed future
cutoffs. Their exact full-precision values are retained in the plan.

The broader 43/64 selections are not qualified for automatic admission. They
retain known unresolved cases, and the current source review leaves 22 Food
and twenty Tools selections unreviewed. The reviewed portion already includes
three new Tools rejections and additional uncertainty. Their larger selection
counts must not be reported as a measured coverage gain.

### Source review and stricter-boundary result

The frozen review selection includes every stricter-boundary selection, both
top-twenty lists, ten cases immediately at or below each boundary, and the
previously diagnosed controls. After deduplication this gives 86 root/note
cases: 31 Food and 55 Tools. Nineteen had no earlier judgment for that root;
fifteen previously reviewed cases have a different winning entry. Posts can
occur under both roots, so these are not 86 independent or necessarily distinct
post samples.

Every selected case retains its full post, full winning meaning, other local
entries, closest actual reference members, score, previous rank and previous
assessment. All stricter-boundary selections receive a source review:

| Root | Selected notes | Straightforward plausible | Qualified plausible | Agent-assessed unsupported |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Food | 10 | 8 | 2 | 0 |
| Tools | 34 | 29 | 4 | 1 |

These are 44 distinct selected notes, with no applied additions. Forty-two
already had a root/note assessment; only two are newly reviewed selections.
Both are Tools cases: Artificial intelligence `L04376`, about AI in propaganda,
is plausible; Sports content solutions `L05323` is assessed unsupported below.
The fresh-case result is therefore one plausible and one unsupported, not an
independent 43/44 accuracy estimate or a measured population error rate.

Food's qualified selections are Seasonings in a legal/branding story and
Caffeine without an identified preparation. Its new Burgers winner removes
the earlier generic-Knives qualification. Tools retains qualifications on
Protocols, a sparse AI/economics post, GB Studio's palette association, and
Figurative tools. Switching a game-construction note's winner to Video games
removes its earlier physical-versus-simulated-construction qualification.
No new human approvals or whole-meaning containment certifications are implied.

### Fresh counterexample: a publisher tagline

The source post, verbatim:

> Resilient Kentucky meets Ole Miss for morning tip-off – Field Level Media – Professional sports content solutions https://www.rawchili.com/nba/557782/ #Basketball #CollegeBasketball #ncaa #NCAABasketball #ncaab
>
> https://media.channels.im/media_attachments/files/115/950/307/882/593/740/original/581d4bd38f2c53d5.jpg

Its new winning entry is **Sports content solutions `L05323`**:

> Sports content solutions are products or services designed to meet needs for creating, supplying, managing, or distributing media content about sports.

That entry scores `0.03964917`, ranking 33 for Tools, above the unchanged
stricter boundary `0.03887072`. It was rank 116 under the ratio method and had
no earlier Tools source assessment. The actual publisher entry, Field Level
Media `L05322`, scores `0.01088093`, and Basketball `L05321` scores `0.01045055`.
The generalized functional definition thus scores about 3.64 times higher than
the publisher's own meaning and 3.79 times higher than Basketball.

I assess this proposed Tools route as unsupported: the source is a basketball
preview carrying publisher branding; it does not discuss a tool or a method
for producing media. This is not a blanket rejection of sports software,
media-production tools or nonsoftware resources. The saved root title or generated
definition is not treated as a fixed admission boundary. The assessment follows
the source role and actual member evidence, and remains open to human review.

The vector sees a functional products/services definition. It does not see the
original post that identifies the phrase as publisher wording. This is a
specific missing distinction, rather than proof that one particular token or
upstream prompt caused the result. No extraction or Bootstrap fault is assigned
by this test. The boundary is not raised after discovering the counterexample.

### What improved and what remains unresolved

Performance struggles, the earlier NBA example `L02909`, drops from Tools rank
28 to 96. Software/device subjects move upward: Servers rises 23 to 5, the
digital-avatar platform rises 40 to 7, and AI models rises 42 to 18. These
measured rank changes agree with the earlier denominator diagnosis.

They do not establish an ending method. The stricter Food boundary has no new
source cases above it, so passing that portion supplies little fresh evidence.
The stricter Tools boundary fails its newly exposed publisher-tagline case.
The broader boundaries also admit known uncertainty. These frozen-boundary tests
do not validate an automatic ending rule. The source counterexample is not
repaired by the ranking improvement alone.

The assistant proposed a probe pooling the explicit meaning with the original
post supplied as encoder context, using this publisher case and genuine positive
controls. The prior attribution of that proposal to the user was incorrect and
was explicitly corrected. It would add the source distinction the
current vectors lack, while retaining the frozen SSE model and excluding LLMs
and training. Additional encoded tokens and input-length limits would need to
be measured before a corpus-wide change. That small new-encoding probe is
recommended for separate authorization, not started here. A different context
is a hypothesis to test, not an asserted fix or a new production policy.

## Original-post context with unchanged full-meaning pooling

The user authorized a small frozen-SSE probe after the assistant described
appending each entry's original post to its existing encoder input. This is an
assistant proposal, not an earlier user suggestion. It tests representation
only; definitions, root memberships, scoring and judgments remain fixed. No
LLM requests, training, paid resources or hierarchy changes are involved.

### Frozen comparison

The new text is exactly:

```text
{title}

{unchanged full explicit meaning}

Original post:
{unchanged full source post}
```

Only the explicit meaning's tokens are pooled. Both candidate and reference
entries receive their own source post. Root references are the actual original
entries, never synthetic-root vectors. The score remains the arithmetic mean
of `exp(-5.5 * (1 - cosine))` over each root's references, excluding every
reference from the candidate's source note.

The fixed panel contains ten Food cases, ten Tools cases and two unassessed
same-post companions for the sports-publisher case. It deliberately includes
known useful and misleading matches, two qualified Food cases and two unresolved
Tools cases. Prior human judgments concern the source notes for culinary Eggs,
egg decoration and the Easter broadcast; they do not establish universal entry
or full-meaning labels. Other judgments remain provisional agent assessments.
This is a selected development panel, not a representative accuracy sample.

Food has 175 original references. One, `L00575` Lunch, would require 532 tokens
with its full post and therefore exceeds the frozen 512-token limit. It is
excluded from both baseline and changed comparisons, without truncation. All
91 Tools references fit. The comparison therefore uses 174 Food and 91 Tools
references. Two diagnostic queries are already reference entries, so there are
285 distinct context inputs rather than 287. The longest retained input is
466 tokens.

The native production worker encodes those 285 inputs and nine exact baseline
replays. All other baseline vectors are reused from their source-bound saved
receipts. The checkpoint, tokenizer, Transformers version and projection are
unchanged. The available host CPU runtime now has Torch `2.14.0+cpu`, versus
`2.13.0+cpu` in the saved baseline. All nine exact replays pass the frozen
`1e-5` absolute-vector and `1e-6` cosine-error limits: maximum absolute error
`2.9802322387695312e-8`, maximum cosine error `5.1181281435219717e-14`.
Execution reserves four of this allocation's 28 logical CPUs and uses the
remaining 24. The retained container label is historical configuration identity;
the actual execution is local host CPU, not a container or rented GPU.

3.3%. It still matches broad Tools and software-related meanings; its nearest

dependent pair counts only describe the selected panel, not accuracy or a
validated stopping method.

The frozen supplementary calculations distinguish candidate-context and
reference-context changes. Adding context only to candidates fixes the one
Food ordering, while changing both sides does not. This does not qualify that
asymmetric representation for deployment: the residual gap is small, the
panel was selected for development, and no new stopping rule was tested. All
four variants and their individual reference connections are retained.

### Scaling and bounded conclusion

For the 285 distinct retained inputs, token count grows from 11,168 to 51,250:
`4.5890` times the baseline, or 358.9% more tokens. This measures encoder input
tokens only, not elapsed-time or API-cost growth. Attention work and batch
padding also depend on length; no timing multiplier is inferred. Posts are
repeated for each entry, so this overhead matters at corpus scale even though
the diagnostic uses local CPU without paid inference.

This small result does not justify adopting post context. It improves one known
misleading match while leaving the motivating publisher case and a human-reviewed
Food ordering unresolved, with substantially greater input length. The current
representation and hierarchy remain unchanged. A useful next investigation is
to inspect the residual nearest-member connections and determine whether the
unchanged mean-affinity score can separate useful routes from misleading ones;
that is a numerical/evidence question, not permission for LLMs or training.

## Original members behind the residual score overlaps

The user authorized tracing the remaining matches to original root members
using saved vectors and judgments. This calculation changes no representation,
meaning, root, reference, judgment or scoring rule. It makes no new encodings,
LLM calls or training runs. The sports-publisher and Wildlife rejection labels
remain provisional agent judgments; the egg-decoration source note has prior
human acceptance. Root names and synthetic definitions are not fixed admission
boundaries, and membership in another root is not negative evidence.

The frozen packet covers all 22 preceding context-probe queries and all twelve
previously reviewed plausible or qualified Tools entries scoring below Sports
content solutions. This exhaustive selection within that saved review avoids
selecting only one favorable contrast. Twelve extra entries have no contextual
encoding, so only their baseline results are calculated. In total there are 34
query entries, 112 root/query results and 14,870 individual reference affinities.
Eight fixed pair contrasts yield eleven baseline/context decompositions where
both representations already exist. Every original-member connection is saved,
including titles, fixed meanings, source-note IDs and full posts.

### Food: one strong member match changes a near-tie

With the current meaning representation and all 175 Food references, Wildlife
scores `0.01574462`, while the human-accepted egg-decoration entry `L02984`
scores `0.01505894`. The gap is `0.0006856848`.

The largest contribution to that gap is one original member:

> `L02407` Food: Food is edible material eaten or intended to be eaten by
> people or animals for nourishment.

Its source begins "Food flying everywhere" and links a food video. It is not
an incorrect Food reference. Its meaning vector nevertheless has cosine
`0.66572` to Wildlife, versus `0.29464` to the decorated-Eggs meaning. Its
contribution to Wildlife-minus-Eggs is `0.0007907874`, greater than the entire
gap. The other 174 contributions sum to `-0.0001051026`, favoring Eggs. Removing
that same reference from both calculations would therefore reverse the order.
This is an explanation of concentration, not authorization or evidence for
deleting it, trimming every nearest match, or changing a meaning.

The Wildlife meaning describes non-domesticated animals, including fish. The
Eggs meaning describes reproductive bodies, embryos, shells and decoration.
Those exact fixed meanings and their source posts remain in the evidence.
The numeric comparison establishes a similarity mismatch under the retained
assessments, not entailment or the word-level cause of an encoder result.

G027, the initial general-Food group, contributes 42.72% of Wildlife's total
affinity; the 38 initially ungrouped references contribute another 20.82%.
For Eggs, G027 contributes 42.80%, Cooking 14.16%, and preservation 6.06%.
The strongest egg-supporting originals include century-egg Food and preservation
mixtures. Thus the match has real localized support but is not separated by
the whole-root average. The strong Wildlife reference supplies only 5.77% of
its total affinity, while still determining which side of this near-tie wins.

The same one-reference sensitivity remains with post context: among the matched
174 references, Wildlife's lead is `0.0000497779`, while `L02407` contributes
`0.0006528137` of that lead. All remaining contributions favor Eggs in total.
The changed-context result uses 174 rather than 175 references because the
earlier Lunch input exceeded 512 tokens; the matched-baseline replay is retained
separately so this exclusion is not attributed to a context effect.

### Tools: the publisher match is spread across the root

Sports content solutions scores `0.03964917`. Its nearest original member is:

> `L04060` Tools: Tools are implements or resources designed or used to help
> carry out tasks or achieve practical goals, including but not limited to
> physical implements, software and informational resources serving that function.

The source promotes a manifesto as "Sovereign tools for sovereign builders."
The query itself defines products or services for creating, supplying, managing
or distributing sports media. Original Software backends, Software dependencies,
Software tools and Software agents also match. These are broad functional
connections to actual members, not an accidental match to a synthetic root.
This evidence does not independently settle the publisher's semantic rejection;
that assessment is not human gold and cannot silently become a scope rule.

One nearest reference supplies only 2.82% of the publisher's total affinity;
the nearest ten together supply 21.31%. Technology contributes 21.61%, Software
11.02%, and the initially ungrouped cohort 24.52%. The result is distributed
across multiple original groups and notes.

Several previously reviewed useful routes still score lower:

| Entry | Prior assessment | Current Tools score | Root members favoring publisher / useful entry |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| Formula 1 upgrades | Plausible | 0.03908263 | 57 / 34 |
| Infrastructure, flood-management works | Plausible | 0.03574713 | 55 / 36 |

| Smartwatches | Plausible | 0.03433156 | 56 / 35 |

These counts describe per-reference affinity comparisons, not votes or a new
decision rule. Publisher-versus-Formula-1 is another near-tie that would reverse
after removing the strongest gap contributor. Publisher-versus-Smartwatches,
Infrastructure, Desks and Reviews still favors the publisher after omitting
the five references most responsible for its lead. Those retrospective omissions
diagnose concentration only and are not a validated pruning rule.

The useful evidence can be concentrated in a smaller portion of the root.
For the camera-review entry, 24.60% of total affinity comes from Testing;
its nearest member is Software comparisons at cosine `0.7173`. Formula 1
upgrades matches Technology tests, Testing and Software updates. Smartwatches
matches Computer peripherals, Technology and Computers. These are not missing
connections: they exist in the saved vectors, but contributions from the rest
of the root can place the broader publisher wording above them.

### What this explains, and what it does not

There are at least two different obstacles to a single whole-root cutoff:
one unusually strong match can change a narrow margin, and broad similarity
across a heterogeneous root can exceed a useful specialized match. The former
does not explain away the latter. Neither nearest-member removal nor changing
the cutoff is justified by these examples. Previous best-raw-group probes also
performed worse on the broader saved panel; this finding does not erase that
negative result or approve switching to the best group.

The next useful numerical test would compare candidate support within each
original group with how that group's own members relate to one another, keeping
source-note exclusions. This would test whether strong support is exceptional
for that particular group, rather than assume that the same raw score has the
same meaning across Cooking, Testing, Technology and Software. It is a proposal
for separate authorization, not a trained classifier, proven ending method or
new production policy. Prior mixed-scope group membership remains evidence,
not semantic gold; inadequate groups must remain explicit rather than be fitted
away. No new test is started here.

## Scaling group matches by internal member similarity

The user authorized testing whether a candidate's similarity to an original
group becomes more useful when compared with that group's own internal
similarity. This is fixed arithmetic over saved full-meaning vectors. No
labels, group membership, meanings, encoder, root definitions or hierarchy
edges change. There are no new encodings, LLM calls, training or paid resources.

### Frozen rule and comparison

Use the existing kernel `K(a,b) = exp(-5.5 * (1 - cosine(a,b)))`.
For a query and each original reference group:

1. Exclude all references from the query's source note.
2. Calculate the mean query-to-reference affinity.
3. Calculate the mean affinity between the remaining references, excluding
   every self-pair and every pair from the same source note.
4. Divide step 2 by step 3. The root's score is the largest such ratio among
   its eligible original groups.

The denominator is a reference similarity scale, not a probability or semantic
label. A group without two independent source notes would remain unavailable,
not receive an invented denominator. No such unavailable group occurs for
these fixed queries. Initially ungrouped references are not combined into a
fabricated group. They remain in the all-member-mean baseline, but not in
either best-original-group method.

Three scores are compared: the unchanged all-member mean, the raw best-group
mean on the same grouped references, and the adjusted best-group mean. Thus
adjustment can be separated from simply selecting a best group. The raw best
group's poor earlier result remains a control, not a discarded approach.
All old-panel baseline counts reproduce exactly before interpreting the new
score.

The frozen older panel has 142 saved root/note assessments; the later panel
has 86. A focused panel contains the preceding twenty cases plus all twelve
previously reviewed useful or qualified Tools cases below the publisher score.
Panels overlap and are not independent samples. All case meanings and winners
remain fixed; no other entry in a note is substituted to improve a method.
Qualified and unresolved assessments remain separate. Most assessments are
provisional agent reviews, not human semantic gold; prior human source-note
decisions and attribution are preserved.

### Broad result: no improvement over the current mean

The table counts useful-versus-agent-assessed-unsupported entry pairs in which
the useful entry scores higher, divided by all such pairs. These are dependent
ordering comparisons, not accuracy, recovered notes or independent trials.

| Saved panel | Root | All-member mean | Raw best group | Group-relative best |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Older 142 | Food | 609 / 609 | 531 / 609 | 587 / 609 |
| Older 142 | Tools | 438 / 456 | 389 / 456 | 382 / 456 |
| Later 86 | Food | 162 / 162 | 134 / 162 | 153 / 162 |
| Later 86 | Tools | 284 / 297 | 255 / 297 | 224 / 297 |
| Focused 32 | Food | 11 / 12 | 12 / 12 | 11 / 12 |
| Focused 32 | Tools | 17 / 22 | 14 / 22 | 17 / 22 |

On the older Tools panel, normalization fixes nine orderings relative to the
current all-member mean but worsens 65. Against the raw best group, it fixes
23 and worsens thirty. Food improves relative to the earlier raw-best-group
method but still loses 22 orderings against the current all-member mean.
The focused panel does not reverse the broader result.

One motivating contrast improves: Smartwatches has adjusted score `0.490880`,
above Sports content solutions at `0.445501`. Both now select G579. The
camera-review case remains below the publisher (`0.298881`), and Wildlife
remains above the human-accepted egg-decoration case (`0.217362` versus
`0.208306`). These scores have a different scale from the original mean; old
cutoffs are not transferred.

### Why dividing by the group scale can backfire

G579 contains only two originals from different notes:

| Entry | Fixed meaning (verbatim) |
|---|---|
| Data cables | Data cables are physical cable assemblies containing a transmission medium designed to carry encoded information between devices or locations, with electrical conductors or optical fibers as possible media. They include, but are not limited to, undersea cables and cables linking locations across the Atlantic. |
| Digital connections | Connections are technical communication links that enable digital systems, applications or services to exchange data or requests, including but not limited to links between a wallet and another application or service. Such links can be provided through different technical mechanisms. |

Their mean kernel affinity is `0.07605354`, the lowest group scale among the
29 tested groups. With two members, there is only one independent cross-note
pair, represented twice as ordered pairs. Its loose connection gives a large
boost after division. G579 becomes the chosen group for 28 of 71 older Tools
cases and 27 of 55 later Tools cases. Across all 180 diagnostic queries scored
against Tools, it wins 127 times; that larger count includes queries not assessed
for Tools and is not a quality statistic.

G153 has two Software agents entries and Operators of software agents, from
only two notes. Its scale is `0.16626857`. The relative score promotes previously
assessed unsupported Staff appointments, Chief executive officers and Taking
orders over several useful technical cases. For example, Device compatibility
scores `0.06050535` versus Staff appointments `0.02627413` under the current
mean. After adjustment, their scores are `0.565256` and `0.586989`, reversing
the retained useful/unsupported ordering. This diagnoses the numeric effect
under prior judgments; it does not assign fault to those original group members
or establish a new human rejection.

Food shows the same scale effect. General-Food G027 has 53 references but an
internal meaning-affinity scale of only `0.10216642`. It becomes the selected
group for 49 of 71 older Food cases and 24 of 31 later cases. The raw group
score had preferred preservation for Eggs, with mean `0.03993250` versus
Wildlife's best raw group mean `0.02350877`. Adjustment instead selects G027
for both, and Wildlife wins. Internal normalization can favor a looser group
over a more specific useful connection; it does not automatically solve the
heterogeneous-root problem.

### The fixed stopping screen also fails

Before calculating results, the plan freezes a score-at-least-one screen:
a candidate must match one group at least as strongly as that group's references
match one another on average. This is a numerical reference condition, not a
claim of semantic validity or a learned threshold.

No case in either the older or later Food panels reaches one, including all
21 and eighteen straightforward-plausible Food cases respectively. The later
Tools panel selects six plausible cases, one qualified case and one previously
assessed unsupported case. The latter is Retrieval (`L01811`), whose source
post is "Hold on let me grab the meme"; it scores `1.203478` via G579. That
rejection remains provisional. The condition is not an established ending rule,
and neither the threshold nor group eligibility is changed after seeing it.

### Conclusion and next evidence

Do not adopt this adjustment. It repairs one selected contrast but makes the
broader saved comparisons worse, and its frozen stopping screen is insufficient.
The experiment's sparse-group sensitivity and the difference between raw and
relative group winners are retained rather than patched with another constant.

The next useful check would examine an assumption behind reusing these groups:
they were formed using title-pooled vectors, while the current admission probes
use full-meaning-pooled vectors. Their coherence under the latter representation
is not guaranteed. Comparing the two internal geometries with saved vectors
would determine whether that mismatch actually explains the weak groups before
proposing another cutoff or regrouping. This is a recommendation only; no new
clustering, representations, model work or hierarchy changes are started here.

## Original-group geometry under title and meaning pooling

The user authorized comparing each original group's internal similarity under
title pooling and full-meaning pooling. This isolates the representation used
by the recent admission probes; it does not change HiGen V5.3, rerun clustering,
change group members or make new semantic judgments. All vectors are reused,
with no new encodings, LLM calls, training or paid resources.

### Source and matched inputs

The 29 original groups contain 204 entries: 137 Food and 67 Tools references.
Every represented group is present in full, not partially sampled. Initially
ungrouped entries have no original group to evaluate and are excluded from
this geometric consistency check. The encoder sees identical
`title + "\n\n" + explicit meaning` text in both modes. Only the pooled tokens
differ: title tokens versus full-meaning tokens, from the same saved forward
pass, checkpoint and tokenizer.

The production hierarchy code's `_vector_key` and `_prepare_encoding` select
the title's byte span. This is also checked against the actual original
native encoding and the normalized vectors submitted to HDBSCAN. The
production saved-vector reader recovers all 204 original title vectors;
the later saved title rows replay them within maximum raw error
`1.043081283569336e-7` and cosine error `1.0380585280245214e-13`. The original
HDBSCAN payload matches the recovered normalized originals within
`5.551115123125783e-17`. The mismatch is therefore pooling choice, not the wrong
source vectors or a new model draw.

For each group, save every distinct-source-note pair's cosine and the unchanged
kernel affinity. For each member, exclude all references from its source note
throughout its root's grouped references. Record its nearest reference and
rank the original groups by average kernel affinity. This second check matters:
uniformly lower cosines alone would not show that relative neighborhoods changed.
Different groups are geometric contrasts, not semantic negative labels.

### Internal similarity and relative neighborhoods both change

All 29 groups have lower mean internal cosine under meaning pooling. Across
groups, the median mean cosine falls from `0.973410` to `0.746523`. This is a
descriptive representation comparison, not an admission threshold or accuracy.

| Root | Members | Nearest reference in own group, title | Nearest reference in own group, meaning | Own group has highest mean, title | Own group has highest mean, meaning |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Food | 137 | 137 | 116 | 137 | 96 |
| Tools | 67 | 67 | 53 | 67 | 46 |
| Total | 204 | 204 | 169 | 204 | 142 |

Thus 35 members change to a nearest reference in another group, and 62 prefer
another group's mean. These are changes relative to existing group labels on
the same references, not 35 or 62 proven semantic errors. Perfect title-mode
consistency is an in-sample property of groups originally formed in that
representation, not evidence of general admission accuracy. Most members still
have a nearest reference within their original group under meaning pooling.

Selected groups illustrate the range; complete results for all 29 are saved:

| Group | Entries | Mean cosine, title | Mean cosine, meaning | Members whose own group has highest mean under meaning pooling |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| G027 — general Food | 53 | 0.973410 | 0.536439 | 21 |
| G579 — Data cables / Digital connections | 2 | 0.806224 | 0.531579 | 1 |
| G153 — Software agents / Operators | 3 | 0.978424 | 0.665004 | 3 |
| G504 — Tests / Testing / Experiments | 9 | 0.868480 | 0.656607 | 7 |
| G172 — Computer code | 3 | 0.995396 | 0.958534 | 3 |
| G648 — Software development | 3 | 0.997232 | 0.915990 | 3 |

### Concrete changes behind the weak scales

G579's two members are each other's nearest references under title pooling.
With meaning pooling, Digital connections (`L06734`) instead has Software
applications (`L06458`, G058) as its nearest reference, at cosine `0.732648`.
Data cables (`L06061`) is only its 21st-nearest remaining grouped Tools reference,
at cosine `0.531579`. Its original group ranks sixth by mean affinity.
Data cables still has Digital connections as its nearest reference: the
similarity is symmetric, but their alternative neighbors differ. Treating
this two-entry set as an interchangeable reference group in the new representation
is therefore a consequential assumption, not an unchanged geometric fact.

General-Food G027 also changes substantially. Under title pooling all 53 members
prefer their own group by both checks. Under meaning pooling 47 still have a
nearest reference in G027, but only 21 prefer its mean over other original
groups. This agrees with the preceding dilution finding: a related individual
reference can remain close while the broad group's mean becomes weak.

Software agents is an important counterexample to a complete mismatch diagnosis.
All three G153 members retain their own group as both nearest-reference group
and best mean under both representations, despite its lower internal cosine.
This group nevertheless contributed to bad relative-score orderings in the
preceding probe. Pooling mismatch therefore does not explain every normalization
failure, and lower internal affinity alone does not establish that a group is
wrong or should be split. Computer code and Software development likewise
retain their relative neighborhoods with much smaller internal changes.

### Bounded conclusion and next test

The original title-based grouping is not geometrically interchangeable with
the meaning-based admission representation. This supports checking that
assumption before assigning a universal meaning-space threshold to the old
groups. It does not establish that meaning vectors are better or worse overall,
that original memberships are semantically invalid, or that new grouping would
solve admission. The source definitions, retained judgments and current hierarchy
remain fixed.

The next useful experiment would rebuild comparison groups with the same
representation used for scoring, alongside a title-pooling control using the
same frozen reference pool and clustering settings. Then repeat the saved
useful/misleading controls. This would test actual admission behavior, rather
than adopt groups because their internal geometry looks better. It is a proposal
only: no reclustering or new admission method is run or activated here.

### Retained evidence

```
target/food-halves-v1/group-pooling-geometry-v1/
```

  controls and geometric checks; SHA256
  `a3a6222cbe865fd8e701e736b3e6addcdea4c6c9b3ca6a764c274c6179341c9b`.

  unchanged original titles, meanings, full posts and native row provenance.

  unordered cross-note pairs and all 29 group summaries in both modes.

## Matched title-versus-meaning reclustering of fixed references

The user authorized regrouping the same saved references under title and
meaning pooling with identical clustering settings, then repeating the saved
admission comparisons. This is a numerical diagnostic, not a HiGen run or new
canonical membership policy. No definitions, roots, source-note judgments,
hierarchy edges or vector contents change. No LLMs, new encodings, training or
paid resources are used.

### Frozen controls and native execution

Both arms use exactly the preceding 204 originally grouped references: 137
Food entries and 67 Tools entries. Each root is clustered separately. The
original 62 initially ungrouped root references are absent from both clustering
arms. Both arms preserve input ordering and opaque entry IDs; only the selected
saved vectors differ. The generic production candidate request builder binds
each vector to its native row, source text and selected byte span. Four native
HDBSCAN calls use `min_samples=3`, `min_cluster_size=2`, excess of mass, the
same frozen angular-distance executable, and six CPU threads each within
disjoint affinity sets. Four logical CPUs remain reserved.

The old executable rejects the compact-output flag. Four first attempts and
their stderr are retained, followed by four successful normal-output invocations
using the existing production compatibility behavior. Request bytes and
clustering settings stay fixed. These are local numerical processes, not
provider attempts. Native membership validation checks each result's input
identities, source hashes, algorithm/configuration and nonoverlapping groups.

The original groups remain a baseline because shrinking the clustering pool
can itself change a density result. Each grouping is scored with both title
and meaning vectors. The primary comparison fixes meaning pooling for candidate
and reference scoring, changing only the new title-derived versus meaning-derived
groups. Raw best-group means and the preceding relative-group ratio are both
retained, with the same source-note exclusion and independent-note eligibility.
All groups are usable here; no unresolved denominator is hidden. The original
all-root mean remains a separate operational baseline using all 175 Food or
91 Tools originals, including the 62 absent from the clustering pool. Its
different reference coverage is explicit; it is not a matched grouping-only
ablation.

### Grouping changes and reference losses

| Root | Clustering vectors | New groups | References assigned / supplied | Source notes with an assigned reference / supplied |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Food | Title | 15 | 137 / 137 | 76 / 76 |
| Food | Meaning | 13 | 63 / 137 | 47 / 76 |
| Tools | Title | 13 | 64 / 67 | 53 / 55 |
| Tools | Meaning | 7 | 41 / 67 | 37 / 55 |

The title control reproduces all fifteen original Food groups. Twelve original
Tools groups remain exact, while one changes and three references become
unassigned. The meaning arm retains 104 of 204 references in groups, leaving
74 Food and 26 Tools entries unassigned. Those losses are part of the tested
group-based scoring, not permission to delete memberships or notes from the
actual hierarchy. Every omitted entry and source-note ID is preserved.

### Ranking improves against the matched group control, not the existing mean

The following uses meaning pooling for scoring throughout. Counts are useful
entries scoring above agent-assessed unsupported entries, divided by all such
dependent pairs. They are not accuracy, independent trials or recovered notes.
The panels overlap and retain their original qualified/unresolved judgments.

| Saved panel | Root | Original-group raw best mean | Reclustered-title raw best mean | Reclustered-meaning raw best mean | Existing all-root mean |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Older 142 | Food | 531 / 609 | 531 / 609 | 583 / 609 | 609 / 609 |
| Older 142 | Tools | 389 / 456 | 389 / 456 | 415 / 456 | 438 / 456 |
| Later 86 | Food | 134 / 162 | 134 / 162 | 152 / 162 | 162 / 162 |
| Later 86 | Tools | 255 / 297 | 259 / 297 | 267 / 297 | 284 / 297 |
| Focused 32 | Food | 12 / 12 | 12 / 12 | 11 / 12 | 11 / 12 |
| Focused 32 | Tools | 14 / 22 | 14 / 22 | 16 / 22 | 17 / 22 |

On the older Food panel, meaning-derived groups fix 53 raw-score orderings
against the matched title groups and worsen one. Tools fixes 39 and worsens
thirteen. The later panel likewise improves in aggregate. The title-scoring
crossed controls and all individual ordering changes are retained, rather than
selecting the best representation after viewing labels.

The relative-group score also improves for Tools against its matched title
control, from 371 to 428 of 456 older-panel orderings, but remains below the
existing mean's 438. Food's relative score declines from 587 to 568 of 609.
Thus regrouping does not rescue normalization generally. No threshold or
parameter is adjusted after seeing this result.

### Specific gains and unresolved losses

With meaning-derived groups and raw mean scoring:

- Smartwatches scores `0.05736364`, just above Sports content solutions at
  `0.05651039`; the publisher rejection remains provisional agent judgment.
- The camera-review entry scores `0.08827715`, above the publisher. However,
  the NBA performance-struggles entry scores `0.10019421`, matching the same
  Testing group, so that retained unsupported example remains stronger.
- Human-accepted egg decoration scores `0.02625279`, below Wildlife at
  `0.02769534`. The raw title-group control had kept Eggs above Wildlife.

The meaning arm leaves century-egg Food (`L06359`) and the food-preservation
mixture (`L00647`) unassigned, losing references that previously supported
Eggs. Food flying everywhere (`L02407`), responsible for the earlier Wildlife
near-tie, also becomes unassigned. Removing that one problematic match therefore
does not resolve the ordering when useful support is lost as well.
Data cables, Digital connections and Operators of software agents also become
unassigned; the improved scores do not demonstrate that these were invalid
reference entries. Root membership and the broader collection's scope remain
unchanged. No new human approval or rejection is inferred.

The previously frozen relative-score-at-least-one screen selects zero cases
in all six meaning-scored/reclustered panel-root combinations, including useful
cases. It is still not a usable ending rule. Better consistency of grouping
and scoring does not by itself establish an admission boundary.

### Conclusion and next control

Matching the representations helps the group-based ranking relative to a
matched title-clustering control. It does not justify adoption: many references
become unassigned, existing all-root means remain stronger on the broad saved
panels, and the fixed stopping screen admits nothing. No admission or hierarchy
rule changes.

The next useful control would retain exactly the entries grouped by the meaning
arm, preserve their previous grouping in one arm and use their new grouping in
the other, then rescore. This would isolate regrouping from simply discarding
references. Current results deliberately report both effects together as the
native clustering outcome; they do not attribute all gains to improved group
geometry. That follow-up was subsequently authorized and is reported in the
equal-reference control below.

## Equal-reference control: dropping references versus regrouping

This authorized follow-up uses only saved vectors, memberships and assessments.
There are no new encodings, HDBSCAN runs, LLM calls, training, thresholds or
graph changes. Meaning pooling and the kernel `exp[-5.5 * (1 - cosine)]` remain
fixed. All source-note exclusions, saved query entries, assessment qualifications
and human note-level judgments remain unchanged.

The three arms separate two operations:

1. **Original full:** the original 29 groups, containing 204 references.
2. **Original retained:** intersect each original group with the references
   retained by meaning-based reclustering. This leaves 63 Food and 41 Tools
   references, in fifteen Food and ten Tools groups.
3. **Meaning retained:** the new thirteen Food and seven Tools groups, using
   exactly those same 104 references.

The first transition measures dropping references with the original grouping
preserved. The second measures regrouping with the reference pool fixed.
The original groups, rather than the preceding newly reclustered title groups,
provide the baseline: title reclustering itself had dropped three Tools entries.
Using that arm would not guarantee the requested identical retained pools.

### Keep singleton references visible

Pruning creates four single-entry original-group subsets: Dining experiences
`L05004` in `G498`, Lunch `L00390` in `G513`, Diets `L00307` in `G566`, and
Software developers `L03051` in `G171`. The main control takes the largest mean
over every nonempty group, including these subsets. Otherwise the two retained
arms would not make the same 104 references available. Their contribution is
not silently assigned a group scale or merged into another group.

The prior raw/relative method requiring two source notes remains a separately
reported sensitivity check. Raw means are available for singleton groups, but
their cross-note internal normalization is undefined. All per-group values and
unavailable scales are retained. None of the 180 saved queries shares a source
note with these retained references; source-note exclusions still apply.

### Attribution results

The main table holds full-meaning scoring fixed and uses every nonempty group.
Counts are useful entries scoring above agent-assessed unsupported entries,
divided by all such dependent pairs. These are not accuracy or notes recovered;
the panels overlap. Qualified and unresolved assessments remain separate in
the saved results. The existing mean uses all 266 original Food/Tools references,
including 62 initially ungrouped references, so it is an operational comparison
with a different reference pool, not a matched aggregation control.

| Saved panel | Root | Original full groups | Original groups, retained references | New meaning groups, same references | Existing all-root mean |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Older 142 | Food | 531 / 609 | 540 / 609 | 583 / 609 | 609 / 609 |
| Older 142 | Tools | 389 / 456 | 421 / 456 | 415 / 456 | 438 / 456 |
| Later 86 | Food | 134 / 162 | 134 / 162 | 152 / 162 | 162 / 162 |
| Later 86 | Tools | 255 / 297 | 266 / 297 | 267 / 297 | 284 / 297 |
| Focused 32 | Food | 12 / 12 | 12 / 12 | 11 / 12 | 11 / 12 |
| Focused 32 | Tools | 14 / 22 | 13 / 22 | 16 / 22 | 17 / 22 |

For older Food, dropping references fixes 28 orderings and worsens nineteen;
regrouping then fixes 44 and worsens one. For older Tools, dropping references
fixes forty and worsens eight; regrouping fixes two and worsens eight. The later
panel has the same broad distinction: Food gains eighteen net orderings from
regrouping, while Tools gains only one. These are descriptions of the saved
panels, not a claim that the removed references were invalid.

Singleton handling explains much of Food's apparent regrouping advantage.
Under the earlier two-source-note eligibility rule, the original retained Food
group score is already 578 / 609, compared with 583 / 609 after regrouping;
the three singleton references are unavailable in that sensitivity check.
Its 540-to-578 difference is therefore explicitly an additional reference
eligibility effect, not evidence that the representations changed. Tools is
420 / 456 versus 415 / 456 under the same restriction. The primary exact-pool
comparison and this restricted sensitivity check must not be conflated.

### The difficult controls remain mixed

- Human-accepted egg decoration `L02984` stays above Wildlife after references
  are dropped but original groups are kept (`0.02302965` versus `0.02128154`).
  Regrouping reverses that ordering (`0.02625279` versus `0.02769534`).
  This focused regression is caused by regrouping, not reference loss alone;
  Wildlife's rejection remains an agent assessment.
- Smartwatches stays below the sports publisher after dropping references
  (`0.05907641` versus `0.07009350`). Regrouping puts it just above the publisher
  (`0.05736364` versus `0.05651039`). This gain does come from regrouping.
- Camera Reviews and NBA Performance struggles both select Testing. Dropping
  references raises their scores to `0.08827715` and `0.10019421` respectively;
  regrouping leaves both unchanged. That overlap is not fixed by the new groups.

Relative normalization also remains mixed. Older Food is 587, 598 and 568 of
609 across the three arms; Tools is 382, 434 and 428 of 456. The unchanged
relative-score-at-least-one screen selects no cases in either retained arm
across all six panel/root combinations. No new stopping threshold is fitted.

### Conclusion and evidence

The preceding apparent improvements had different causes. Regrouping helps
Food on the broad panels, partly by changing how tiny retained groups affect
the maximum. Most of Tools' gain comes from dropping references, and its
regrouping effect is small or negative outside the focused controls. These
findings do not support replacing the existing all-root mean or deleting the
100 references left unassigned by meaning reclustering. They do not establish
an ending method. The synthetic root definitions remain outside this test.

More regrouping changes are not warranted by this control alone. Before
optimizing further against Sports content solutions, the useful next decision
is whether that source should actually be rejected from the broad Tools
collection. Its current rejection is provisional agent judgment; the numerical
diagnosis cannot turn it into human gold. This is a recommendation only, not
a new review or calculation started here.

## Title/meaning neighborhood fusion

The user authorized testing whether title and meaning neighbor relationships
can be combined more usefully than an arithmetic cosine blend. This bounded
probe reuses the original 137 Food and 67 Tools references, both saved vector
representations and the same 180 query entries. It makes no new encodings,
LLM calls, training runs, semantic reviews, hierarchy changes or UI updates.
Only reference grouping changes; candidate scoring stays in meaning space.

### Frozen calculation and controls

The experimental module is `sse_multivector/similarity_network_fusion.py`.
It follows the exchange and normalization equations of the authors'
[SNFtool implementation](https://github.com/maxconway/SNFtool/blob/master/R/SNF.R),
using neighbor count twenty, local scale multiplier `0.5` and twenty iterations.
Those settings were frozen before scoring and were not tuned on the judgments.
This is a **bounded sparse adaptation**, not an exact implementation of dense
published SNF:

1. Build separate title and meaning networks from angular distances, with
   locally scaled Gaussian affinities on mutual twenty-neighbor connections.
2. Normalize each network. Update the title network by passing the meaning
   network through title-neighbor transitions, and update meaning through title
   information simultaneously. Add identity mass and symmetrize each round.
3. Limit each state to twenty mutual non-self connections per entry. Prune the
   first intermediate product before the second multiplication, preventing
   unbounded dense diffusion. The final graph has the same degree limit.
4. Extract EOM groups using HDBSCAN on reciprocal nonzero edge affinities.
   Missing connections remain infinite distances, with no invented edge.
   Minimum cluster size is two; minimum samples is three including the entry.

Two matched controls use identical inputs and graph machinery: **Unfused** has
zero diffusion iterations; **Self diffusion** runs twenty iterations but each
representation exchanges only with itself. The latter distinguishes mixing
the representations from diffusion alone. All resulting group memberships,
noise entries, warnings and intermediate sparsity counts remain saved.

The existing native angular decoder does not accept an arbitrary fused network.
The numerical probe therefore uses scikit-learn's precomputed-distance HDBSCAN.
Angular-distance replay reproduces both saved title memberships exactly. Meaning
replays differ by three Food and four Tools entries: 66 versus 63 and 45 versus
41 assigned entries respectively. Both native and library-reference controls
are retained separately. This implementation difference is not attributed to
the representation or declared a semantic error. An amendment recording these
controls was frozen before query scoring; no clustering settings changed.
Unfused, self-diffusion and exchanged-fusion comparisons share the same decoder.

### Reference retention and ranking

Every arm starts with the same 204 references. The outputs retain different
subsets; these counts describe the complete grouping method, not a pure
regrouping effect with identical retained references.

| Root | Native title | Native meaning | Unfused | Self diffusion | Exchanged fusion |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Food, 137 entries | 137 | 63 | 108 | 125 | 111 |

Six new production-path tests first failed for the missing module, then passed

secondary relative score divides that mean by the group's cross-source-note
