## Combining outside title and meaning against fixed accepted connections

The user authorized testing both outside vectors together while preserving the
accepted reference. All 25 groups, 177 accepted reference entries, original
fused weights, source meanings, query entries and saved assessments remain
unchanged. No outsider is included in fusion, HDBSCAN or a reference vector set.
No accepted entry is removed or redefined. There are no new model calls,
encodings, clustering runs, training, graph placements or UI updates.

### Frozen query rule and controls

The experimental `sse_multivector/fixed_reference_query.py` computes support
from the two query vectors and the already-saved accepted connections. For each
group, let `t_i` be the outsider's title affinity to accepted entry `i`, `m_j`
its meaning affinity to accepted entry `j`, and `C_ij` their fixed connection.
Both affinities use the unchanged kernel `exp[-5.5 * (1 - cosine)]`.

The combined score is:

```
sqrt(sum(C_ij * t_i * m_j) / sum(C_ij))
```

Self-connections and connections between entries from the same source note are
excluded. All entries from the query's own source note are also excluded. This
lets title and meaning match different accepted entries when those entries have
a saved connection. No outside connection is written back. Every query is
independent of other queries, and swapping its two views preserves the score
because the accepted connections are symmetric.

Matched single-view controls substitute `t_i * t_j` or `m_i * m_j` into exactly
the same weighted calculation. A separate control combines both views at each
same reference entry, `sqrt(mean(t_i * m_i))`, without accepted connections.
It uses the same eligible groups. This is the stated square-root-of-mean-product
control, not a claim to have tested every arithmetic vector blend.

The primary comparison uses the largest raw group score. Formulas and controls
were frozen before query scores were calculated. No weights, thresholds or
labels are fitted. This custom query rule is not published SNF inference or a
Nyström implementation. The literature confirms that extending a fixed graph
to new inputs is a separate problem, but does not validate this proposed score:
[Bengio et al., 2003](https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper/2003/hash/cf05968255451bdefe3c5bc64d550517-Abstract.html).

semantic accuracy or recovered notes. Qualified and unresolved judgments stay
separate in the saved results.

### Separate reference-level screen

A graph query score cannot reuse the old internal-cosine denominator as if it
were the same measurement. The frozen secondary diagnostic instead scores each
accepted group member with that same query rule, excluding its entire source
note from both eligible references and connection endpoints. The group's
reference level is the mean of all these scores. Graph weights remain fixed;
this is an in-sample reference scale, not an independently held-out semantic
calibration or a fitted classifier.

If any reference member lacks surviving eligible connections, the group has no
relative score. Three two-entry Food groups are unavailable: `fused-012`,
`fused-013` and `fused-014`. The last is the Food pair that supported Milk, Tea
and Water in the earlier diagnostic. After excluding either member's note,
there is no remaining pair to evaluate. Their raw scores remain present; no
missing reference score becomes zero. All methods use the same 22 eligible
groups for this secondary comparison: twelve Food groups and all ten Tools
groups, giving 88 available method/group scales and twelve unavailable ones.

The diagnostic divides each outside group score by its reference level and
takes the largest ratio. Parity at one is tested without tuning, but its scale
definition differs from the preceding cosine/internal-mean cutoff. Selection
counts across those different rules are not a controlled measurement of the
combination alone.

The combined rule selects only Servers `L03830` (`1.045489`) and Software clients
`L04264` (`1.816696`), both through `fused-009`. Both are existing plausible
assessments, and both were already among the earlier ten meaning-only candidate
notes. No new reviewed source note is recovered relative to that earlier set.
The matched title-through-connections control selects Digital platforms and
Software clients; meaning-through-connections selects
nine cases, including one qualified case; the no-connection combination selects
none. All case identities and original assessments are retained.

## Testing local graph boundaries without a constant acceptance score

The user rejected a particular numerical admission cutoff and authorized testing
local graph expansion around the accepted fusion groups. This test uses the same
15 Food groups and ten Tools groups, with 111 and 66 accepted reference entries.
Those memberships, accepted fusion files, meanings and saved case assessments
remain fixed. A separate graph includes all 6,991 eligible original entries,
using only their saved full-meaning SSE vectors. Its outside entries participate
in the new boundary calculation, not in the accepted fusion networks. Nothing
is written back to either network, a canonical node or the original hierarchy.

### Frozen graph and boundary calculation

The existing `similarity_network_fusion.local_graph` constructs mutual
20-neighbor connections with its existing local scale multiplier `0.5`.
Self edges and edges between entries from the same source note are then removed.
As in that existing constructor, same-note entries may consume places during
neighbor retrieval before those edges are removed. The full graph has 72,836
directed non-self edges, 144 connected components and 134 isolated entries.
Its largest component contains 6,784 entries. No dense all-pairs graph is stored.

For each accepted group independently:

1. Give its source notes equal starting mass, divided among their group entries.
2. Compute personalized PageRank with continuation `0.85`; divide by graph degree
   to order nearby entries. The numerical convergence tolerance is `1e-12`, not
   an admission score.
3. Keep all original group entries, then examine progressively larger prefixes
   of that order. Calculate **conductance**: connection weight crossing the set
   boundary divided by the smaller side's total incident connection weight.
4. Select the smallest minimum-conductance prefix among those containing at most
   half the graph's total incident weight. If that constrained minimum has no
   local valley, propose no additions. A seed-only result is also allowed.
5. Union the separate group proposals for each root. Roots can overlap; membership
   in another root never supplies a rejection label.

The preselected secondary control uses the **first local minimum** instead of
the lowest one. It is reported regardless of whether it looks better. Two
continuation controls (`0.5`, `0.95`) and two candidate-pool controls were also
frozen before results. No label selects these settings or boundaries.

This is a seed-preserving adaptation of the conductance sweep described in
[local graph-clustering research](https://cs.stanford.edu/people/jure/pubs/mappr-kdd17.pdf).
It does not inherit an unqualified semantic guarantee from that research. Its
boundary adapts to the graph, but graph construction and diffusion still have
parameters. Numerical clustering is not semantic model training.

### Minimum conductance is much too broad

| Full-pool result | Food | Tools |
|---|---:|---:|
| Original reference entries | 111 | 66 |
| Proposed omitted entries | 1,305 | 1,193 |
| Proposed omitted notes, of 281 eligible | 281 | 278 |
| Later-panel plausible cases selected | 18 / 18 | 28 / 33 |
| Later-panel agent-assessed mismatches selected | 9 / 9 | 9 / 9 |

The primary rule selects all three human-accepted Food control notes, **and the
Easter broadcast `L02222` that the user explicitly rejected**. That is a direct
failure under a human decision, beyond disagreements with provisional agent
assessments. All 177 accepted entries survive the whole-note seed-removal check,
but that retention is uninformative when the rule accepts almost everything.

Twenty-one of 25 group expansions select a resolved minimum; four propose no
additions because the minimum is only a search-limit endpoint. The resolved
boundaries select roughly 4,600–5,000 entries per group. Low-degree vertices
allow that many entries within the half-volume condition. Taking the union
then covers most of the corpus. The calculation finds a broad graph separation,
not a boundary specific enough for the original accepted topic group.

### First local minima are narrower but not a qualified solution

| Full-pool first-minimum result | Food | Tools |
|---|---:|---:|
| Proposed omitted entries | 7 | 8 |
| Proposed omitted notes | 6 | 6 |
| Later-panel plausible cases selected | 2 / 18 | 4 / 33 |
| Later-panel agent-assessed mismatches selected | 0 / 9 | 0 / 9 |
| Withheld accepted entries recovered | 93 / 111 | 43 / 66 |
| Withheld accepted notes recovered | 58 / 69 | 40 / 54 |

The two sets cover twelve distinct omitted notes. Food examples include Burgers,
Coffee shops, Drinking hot tea, Sipping coffee and Spiciness. Tools examples
include GPU hardware, Open-source software, Executable files, networking
configuration, Mesh networks and Formula 1 technical upgrades. The full source
review assesses thirteen of these fifteen entry routes as plausible and two as
qualified: caffeine without an identified food/drink, and a sparse mesh-device
conversation. These are agent assessments, not new human gold. The Advice entry
shares the already-selected burger note, and duplicate entry routes do not add
extra notes. Software clients is selected in the saved panel but its source note
is already reachable elsewhere, so it is not among the twelve omitted notes.

The first-minimum control retains the accepted chocolate/egg-preparation note
through its Chocolate entry, misses the human-accepted egg-yolk/coffee and
pisanki notes, and excludes the human-rejected Easter broadcast. The earlier
panels also contain no selected agent-assessed mismatches under this full-pool
control, but useful-case selection remains low. The overlapping panels are not
independent accuracy estimates, and omitted positives need not all be recovered.

Each accepted-note check removes that entire note from **every seed group of
its root**, rather than hiding one duplicate entry. The graph and original group
discovery remain fixed, so this is a transductive seed-removal control, not an
independent group-discovery test. The fixed outside-panel query notes do not
occur in the seed groups.

### Candidate-pool changes alter the boundaries

The smaller pool contains the 177 references, all 1,426 omitted entries, and the
fixed query controls: 1,607 entries in total. One control rebuilds its graph;
the other restricts the original full graph without changing retained edge
weights. Both preserve the same accepted reference groups.

| First-minimum proposals | Food omitted entries / notes | Tools omitted entries / notes |
|---|---:|---:|
| Full graph | 7 / 6 | 8 / 6 |
| Smaller pool, rebuilt graph | 25 / 20 | 24 / 17 |
| Smaller pool, original retained edges | 25 / 21 | 26 / 18 |

Rebuilding the smaller graph loses one of Food's seven full-pool proposals and
four of Tools' eight, while adding nineteen and twenty others. It also selects
the previously agent-assessed Nausea mismatch; the induced control selects Nausea
and Blood. Those remain agent judgments. This is candidate-pool sensitivity,
not proof that every changed proposal is wrong. Changing continuation alone is
less disruptive, but also changes selected entries. No favorable control is
retrospectively promoted to the primary method.

A further trace identifies an assumption needing attention: **nine of the 25
fusion groups are disconnected within their own entries in this separate
meaning-neighbor graph**. Food `fused-014`, the two-entry Food group, has no
internal edge at all. Tools `fused-009`, the five-entry agents/cables/connections
group, has only one undirected internal edge; 95.2% of its incident connection
weight leaves the seed set. Those are properties of this particular sparse
meaning graph, not proof that the accepted meanings or fusion groups are invalid.
They show that a neighborhood formed by title/meaning fusion is not automatically
a cohesive starting neighborhood for meaning-only graph expansion.

The primary method fails. The first-minimum control is a limited conservative
candidate, with no demonstrated general boundary: local minima omit useful
cases and change with the graph pool. This result motivated tracing one clear
missed fit through its group's full curve and inspecting the group/graph mismatch
before proposing another boundary rule. The subsequent authorized trace below
preserves these inputs and rules.

### Verification, scaling and saved evidence

Six production-path tests first failed because the helper was absent, then pass.
They exercise a hand-calculated weak bridge between neighborhoods, zero-addition
and isolated-seed outcomes, weight-scale invariance, immutable repeated queries
and invalid input rejection. Together with fusion, fixed-query and hierarchy
HDBSCAN regression checks, 29 tests pass.

Independent verification binds all 6,991 normalized vectors to their exact input
text, full-meaning UTF-8 spans and raw receipts; checks 125 graph sweeps, 578,868
cut points, all saved PageRank equations and 2,600 panel membership records; and
preserves 83 protected source hashes. Maximum cut discrepancy is `2.15e-12` and
maximum PageRank residual `9.42e-13`. The initial verifier's strict rank equality
failed on a two-entry tail tie differing by about `1e-23` when division replaced
reciprocal multiplication. That failure is retained. The corrected verifier
checks the exact saved arithmetic order and independent monotonicity; scores,
boundaries, proposals and settings are unchanged.

The base probe and 123 whole-note seed-removal cases take 22.7 seconds locally.
Sparse graph storage is linear in the number of neighbor edges; each diffusion
iteration and sweep is linear in vertices plus edges, with sorting per group.
Existing exact nearest-neighbor retrieval remains quadratic work at corpus scale,
and 25 whole-component sweeps are not a qualified production-scale solution.
No retrieval index, new constructor step or automatic membership policy is added.

## Tracing a clear missed fit: Coffee

The user authorized tracing one suitable missed entry through its existing
group's connections and boundary curve. The case is `L00228`, **Coffee**:

> Coffee is a beverage prepared by extracting roasted coffee beans with water.

Its source post reads "More coffee in cool places" followed by an image URL.
The image was not inspected. Its useful Food route was already assessed as
plausible; this trace neither creates human gold nor changes that assessment.

The group is chosen before inspecting graph positions: `fused-014`, its strongest
Food group under the earlier direct mean-affinity calculation. The same group
also wins its earlier meaning-through-connections raw score. It contains:

| Entry | Fixed explicit meaning |
|---|---|
| `L00445` Food | Food is substances suitable for people to eat or drink and intended to provide nourishment, in solid or liquid form. |
| `L06842` Food | Food encompasses substances and products prepared or used for human consumption by eating or drinking, together with edible ingredients intended for incorporation into those products. It includes but is not limited to protein bars, energy drinks and sweetening ingredients such as the no-calorie sweetener erythritol. |

Both explicitly include drinks. Coffee's cosine similarities to them are
`0.57308` and `0.52327`; its unchanged direct mean affinity is `0.084107`.
The accepted fusion network connects the two Food entries, while the separate
full-corpus meaning-neighbor graph does not. Their weight units differ, so this
is an edge-presence comparison rather than a comparison of weight magnitudes.

### The first minimum stops before further drink-related entries

The saved first-minimum result keeps the two Food entries and eight additions:
Energy drinks, Purelife, Caffeine, Food and drinks, Sweet tea, Dark chocolate,
Food and Drinking. Its next steps are:

| Added position after the two references | Entry | Conductance |
|---:|---|---:|
| 7 | Food | 0.788962 |
| 8 | Drinking — selected first minimum | 0.786004 |
| 9 | Beer | 0.807964 |
| 10 | Diets | 0.783893 |
| 11 | Beer | 0.749093 |
| 12 | Beverages | 0.725352 |
| 13 | Beer | 0.643580 |

Beer causes one increase, enough for the first-minimum control to stop. By
position ten, conductance is already lower than the selected first minimum.
The chosen set still sends 78.6% of its incident connection weight outside,
so a local dip does not by itself establish an isolated topic neighborhood.
No new semantic cutoff is selected from this observation.

Coffee enters at added position **730**, giving a 732-entry prefix. Across all
fifteen Food groups its earliest position is this same 730; no other first-minimum
group would recover it. Its twenty actual mutual meaning-neighbor connections
lead to sixteen Coffee entries plus Coffee brewing, Coffee beans, Coffee
availability and Taste profiles. None is an accepted Food reference. It has
zero connection weight to the selected ten-entry first-boundary set; inserting
Coffee alone there increases conductance from `0.786004` to `0.891479`.

A shortest reciprocal-weight path reaches Coffee through Food, Energy drinks,
Hot beverages, Coffee brewing and another Coffee entry. These are similarity
graph connections, **not hierarchy parent relationships**. The direct Food-to-
Coffee similarities exist in the saved vectors but are absent from the sparse
mutual-neighbor graph. That graph favors the many close Coffee variants and
requires several similarity links to reach the broader Food reference.

### Ordering changes, independently of the selected boundary

A post-trace component control orders exactly the same 6,782 reachable nonseed
entries by their original direct mean affinity to these two Food references.
It keeps the vectors, accepted group, candidate population and kernel unchanged.
This is an ordering comparison only; it does not run a new expansion or select
a new boundary.

| Entry | Direct mean-support position | Saved graph-order position |
|---|---:|---:|
| Coffee `L00228` | 49 | 730 |
| Caffeine `L00225` | 44 | 3 |
| Wildlife `L04627` | 1,622 | 408 |
| Easter broadcast `L02222` | 4,632 | 1,861 |

The Wildlife mismatch remains an agent assessment; the Easter note rejection
comes from the user. These positions are not admission outcomes or precision
estimates. They show that the graph-ordering stage reverses the Coffee/Wildlife
ordering and pushes Coffee far behind its direct meaning-support position.
That stage includes neighbor pruning, local edge scaling, diffusion and degree
normalization. This control does not isolate which individual operation causes
the change and must not be reported as proof that PageRank alone is responsible.

Two concrete obstacles are now visible: the first local minimum mistakes a
brief early increase for an ending, and the graph ordering introduces a long
delay before a clear direct-support match. This initially motivated a proposed
conductance rerun with direct meaning-support ordering. The user's subsequent
decision below rejects using a graph whose connections depend on the outsider
pool, so that rerun is withdrawn. No such rerun was started or claimed successful.

## Approved return to fixed fusion references; adaptive-boundary proposal

The user explicitly decided: "we will stick w the fusion setup and evaluate each
outsider independently. we will not use a fixed cutoff." Accepted fusion groups,
connections and weights remain the reference; another outsider must not replace
their neighbors, rescale their connections, supply a path or move the boundary
used to assess a particular query. Outside-query comparisons continue to use
meaning vectors. The failed large mixed graph and trace remain evidence, not
an admission path. No production generator or existing hierarchy changes.

The admission boundary remains unresolved. The following proposal was tested in
the next section; it is not an adopted admission algorithm:

1. Keep the existing fusion reference and direct mean-affinity query score fixed.
   For an entry `q` and group `G`, average the existing
   `exp[-5.5 * (1 - meaning_cosine(q, r))]` over available members `r` of `G`.
   Exclude every reference from `q`'s source note. Do not compare that score
   directly with a fused edge weight: those measurements have different units.
2. Calculate that same query score for accepted reference entries, using only
   the fixed accepted pool of the same root as context. The current pools used
   to build fusion contain 137 Food and 67 Tools entries; this introduces no
   previously unaccepted reference. Include the group's surrounding accepted
   entries, not only its own members. In particular, a two-entry group supplies
   only one cross-note pair similarity and cannot alone justify a curve bend.
   Other accepted topics are geometric context, not root-level negatives.
3. Form a reference-only support curve, ordering these accepted scores and
   giving each source note equal total weight. Investigate the original bend
   principle: normalize the axes, inspect the central 80%, and find the largest
   departure from the endpoint chord marking the end of the strongly supported
   neighborhood. This must specify and preserve the bend orientation; the old
   outsider-score bend and the hierarchy split-distance bend are not identical
   calculations. No outsider score enters this reference curve. A missing bend
   remains unresolved rather than triggering a hand-selected score or percentile.
4. Check whether the resulting boundary can recover the group's existing
   members with their entire source note excluded from both the reference and
   boundary calculation. A group whose meaning-support geometry cannot support
   such a boundary remains unavailable for automatic expansion; its accepted
   memberships are retained. This internal check is necessary, not semantic
   certification, and does not authorize altering groups or tuning parameters.
5. Evaluate a new outsider against the resulting group-specific boundaries.
   Several groups or roots may provide support; there is no exclusive winner
   or other-root rejection. Boundaries can differ between groups and reference
   versions, but the same query/reference version must produce the same result
   when other outsiders are added, removed, duplicated or reordered.

The distinction from earlier failed ending rules is concrete: those bent a
curve made from the outsiders being selected, or used parity with one reference
average. This proposal uses accepted-reference structure to derive a local
boundary and separately checks whether that structure can support it. It is
still possible for a reference-only bend to give an unsuitable boundary; that
must be measured rather than assumed away. A weak group must not acquire broad
admission simply because its reference scores are low.

HDBSCAN's documented
[fixed-tree prediction](https://hdbscan.readthedocs.io/en/latest/prediction_tutorial.html)
provides precedent for assessing a query without changing the accepted
structure. It does not validate this proposed reference-score estimator or
establish comparable fused/query weight scales. The earlier stock HDBSCAN
prediction check, which retrieved only two notes, is not recast as a success.

The proposed first check is to inspect these reference-only curves with the
saved fit/mismatch cases marked but not used to choose a boundary, then test
the same frozen calculation on Food and Tools. Keep whole-note exclusions,
human decisions, provisional assessments and unresolved cases separate. Check
both useful recovery and unwanted admission; selecting nothing is not success.
Cache reference calculations by their fixed source identity, avoid outsider-to-
outsider comparisons and retain exact invariance tests. Reference curve work is
precomputed; no corpus-wide mixed graph is required. Scaling beyond the current
small reference pools remains unqualified.

That design discussion performed no experiment or hierarchy change. The next
section records the subsequently authorized numerical test separately.

## Fixed accepted-reference bend: measured recovery and failure

The user authorized testing the preceding hypothesis. This is an experimental
saved-vector result, **not an adopted admission rule or a hierarchy update**.
The fixed fusion reference and independent-query constraints remain approved;
an adequate admission boundary remains unresolved.

Frozen artifact directory:
`target/food-halves-v1/fixed-reference-bend-v1/` in the
`oracle-mvp-hierarchy-food-halves-v1` worktree. `plan.json` was written before
outcomes; SHA256
`b470cd59900263ee36384834ddc44359977022b7377cf9c8950e676c7207ab16`.
It binds the saved ASv6.100-derived inputs, original fusion files, full-meaning
vectors, query cases, source code and tests. Generated evidence remains ignored.
Reusable experimental calculation: `sse_multivector/fixed_reference_bend.py`.

The primary calculation preserves the existing mean meaning-kernel score and
fits a separate curve for each fixed group, using only the accepted entries of
its root: 137 Food and 67 Tools entries. Each source note contributes total
curve mass one. Aggregate exact score ties, order support descending, remove
`ceil(10% of distinct score levels)` from each end, require three retained
levels, normalize cumulative note mass and support drop, and select the largest
positive `x-y` departure from the endpoint chord. Place the boundary midway to
the next lower score. No positive bend means no boundary; `1e-12` only suppresses
floating-point artifacts on straight curves, not admission scores.

This is an adaptation of the hierarchy bend orientation to different measured
quantities, not evidence that the same orientation has the same semantic effect.
The opposite `y-x` orientation was frozen as a diagnostic, never substituted
for the primary result after viewing outcomes. Gated and ungated versions were
also frozen. A group is eligible only if its base boundary exists and every
member passes after removing its entire source note from both the group and
accepted curve context. Fusion memberships are held fixed during this check;
it tests self-consistency, not independent group discovery or accuracy.

The test evaluates 1,449 independent entries: all 1,426 eligible entries from
281 actually omitted notes, plus the saved panels and every eligible heading
from the four human-reviewed notes. There are no outsider-to-outsider scores.
The 177 selected group members remain inside their original 204-entry accepted
context pools. No query source note overlaps those pools in this frozen test;
unit tests separately exercise complete source-note removal.

| Primary result | Food | Tools |
|---|---:|---:|
| Fixed fusion groups | 15 | 10 |
| Groups with a positive bend | 9 | 6 |
| Groups passing every whole-note exclusion check | 4 | 2 |
| Proposed entries from actually omitted notes | 104 | 34 |
| Proposed distinct omitted notes | 50 | 20 |

The two roots overlap on three proposed notes: **67 distinct omitted notes**
in total, not 70. These are unimplemented proposals, mostly unreviewed; they
must not be described as 67 correctly recovered notes or added to actual
coverage. HR35 still reaches **718 notes**.

The saved panels contain overlapping, provisional **agent** assessments, not
human gold or independent test samples. Keep direct plausible cases separate
from qualified and unresolved cases:

| Panel | Root | Plausible proposed | Qualified proposed | Mismatches proposed | Unresolved proposed |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Older 142 | Food | 19/21 | 7/9 | 3/29 | 5/12 |
| Older 142 | Tools | 15/38 | 2/15 | 0/12 | 0/6 |
| Later 86 | Food | 16/18 | 2/2 | 0/9 | 2/2 |
| Later 86 | Tools | 16/33 | 2/11 | 0/9 | 0/2 |
| Focused 32 | Food | 5/6 | 2/2 | 0/2 | 0/0 |
| Focused 32 | Tools | 5/11 | 0/7 | 0/2 | 0/2 |

There is a real improvement on the traced Coffee case, but the boundary is not
yet reliable. Food `fused-014`, the two general Food definitions, accepts Coffee
L00228: score `0.084107` versus boundary `0.015549`. The same eligible group
also proposes all three older-panel mismatches: Nausea L02276 (`0.021513`),
Blood L06803 (`0.046273`) and JAIA Drake L04019 (`0.017687`). Its strongest
positive bend has departure only `0.005639` and selects score level 118 of
136, near the low-support end. Recovering its own Food members does not prove
that this remote-tail bend marks an appropriate admission boundary.

The four human judgments are evaluated at **note level**, through any eligible
heading from the note; they do not approve every heading individually:

| Human-reviewed note | User decision | Primary result |
|---|---|---|
| Chocolate/eggs note, example L00101 | Accept | Proposed |
| Coffee with raw cream and egg yolk, example L00440 | Accept | Proposed |
| Pisanki egg-decoration note, example L02984 | Accept | Missed through every heading |
| Religious/political Easter broadcast, example L02222 | Reject | Not proposed |

Tools exposes instability in the boundary calculation, not confirmed semantic
errors among its omissions. Only Computer code and Software projects pass the
full check; broad Technology/Tools and Software groups are disabled.
For Technology/Tools `fused-002`, withholding the note containing Tools L04303
moves the fitted boundary from `0.034834` to `0.287453`. The held Tools member
scores `0.213612` and fails. Software `fused-004` has boundary `0.063039`, but
withholding the Software applications L06458 note leaves no positive bend.
This shows instability under accepted-note removal, rather than proving those
accepted memberships wrong. Disabled groups retain all original memberships.

Removing the self-consistency gate is not a fix: older-panel proposals rise to
20/21 plausible Food cases with 10/29 mismatches, and 32/38 plausible Tools
cases with 4/12 mismatches. The predeclared opposite-orientation diagnostic
passes more groups (13 Food, eight Tools) and matches all four human note
controls, including egg decoration. It still proposes older-panel mismatches
(two Food, one Tools) and three of nine later Tools mismatches. It is not
promoted as a solution.

The conclusion is narrower than rejecting independent fixed-reference scoring:
**a strongest reference-only bend plus self-recovery is insufficient**. A weak
tail bend can pass self-recovery, while removing one accepted note can destroy
an otherwise useful group's bend. The experiment does not justify a new score
cutoff, relaxing the gate, changing orientation after the fact, or returning to
the rejected mixed outsider graph.

Verification preserves the actual calculation and evidence:

- Eight new tests pass through the experimental calculation used by the probe;
  the initial test run failed because that implementation did not yet exist.
  Thirty-one combined boundary/query/fusion/HDBSCAN tests pass.
- All 50 group/orientation models, 362 base/fold boundaries, 38,756 reference
  curve scores and 72,450 query/group scores are independently checked using
  scalar arithmetic. Maximum discrepancy is `3.06e-16`.
- Query reordering, duplication and isolated evaluation produce exactly the
  same scores for all 25 groups and both orientations. Boundaries have no query
  input. The 4,500 earlier direct mean-kernel scores replay within `1.67e-16`.
- Source hashes, accepted fusion files and hierarchy state remain unchanged.
  No new vectors, provider calls, training, paid resources or UI updates occur.

The numerical evaluation takes 3.9 seconds for these small reference pools;
this is not a production scaling qualification. Reference kernels use
`R*R` storage, query scoring uses `Q*R`, and note-exclusion fits are cached.
The straightforward all-fold fitting implementation repeats reference-curve
work and is experimental; it is not installed in hierarchy generation.
Maintained findings, calculation and tests land on local `main`; the task
worktree remains for frozen source paths and ignored evidence.

## Member-anchored reference bend: controlled follow-up

The user subsequently authorized: "try this", quoting the proposal to anchor
boundaries to the group's own members. The test changes the experimental
reference-curve endpoint; it does not change hierarchy generation, accepted
fusion groups or weights, meanings, vectors or the outsider mean-affinity score.
All outsiders remain independent. No model calls, new encodings or training.

used. Historical source bytes remain in Git; the new manifest verifies the
previous code against commit `a30bcdcf` and binds the changed code separately.
Old artifacts remain unchanged.

For each group, calculate its own members' mean meaning-affinity scores against
other source notes in that group. The weakest such score becomes the fitted
curve's upper endpoint, replacing the previous upper 10% trim. Stronger scores
are outside the fit, and the original lower `ceil(10% of full distinct levels)`
trim remains. Recompute the endpoint within every note-exclusion fold. A group
with no cross-note member support has no anchor; no inherited full-data anchor
or numerical cutoff is substituted. Fit the bend as before below that endpoint.

Both orientations, with and without the all-fold self-consistency gate, were

## Fixed fusion neighbors as curve context: controlled follow-up

The user authorized testing whether each reference curve should use the group's
existing fusion neighbors rather than every accepted entry in the root. This
changes the curve population only: fixed groups, graph bytes/weights, meaning
vectors, query mean-affinity scores, note weighting, member anchors, bend
orientation and lower trimming rules are preserved. No query enters a graph.

For each group, include its members and their direct positive-edge neighbors
in the original symmetric fusion graph. No transitive search, new neighbor
count or weight cutoff is added. In a whole-note fold, first remove the held
note's group entries, then take the remaining members' neighbors in that same
unchanged graph, excluding the held note entirely. Graph weights select the
accepted context; they are never compared numerically with meaning scores.
The test keeps both bend directions and gated/ungated variants, comparing each
against exactly the same settings with the full accepted root as context.

integer indices. Preserve its plan, failed source snapshots, stderr and partial
output. A production-path JSON test reproduces that failure; converting indices
to native integers fixes it. The retry has the same selection rule and was
frozen before query outcomes were emitted. No inference, encoding or spending.

`y-x`, no hard self-check, now with direct fusion-neighbor context. Same 1,449
query entries and 281 actually omitted eligible notes; no labels fit a boundary.
The 137-entry Food curve pools shrink, for example, to 28 entries for general
Food `fused-014`, 22 for preservation `fused-008` and seven for Eating `fused-010`.

- All three human-confirmed rejection entries (Nausea L02276, Blood L06803,

- Breath L04399 is no longer proposed. This is **abstention**, not a demonstrated

- Gas facilities L06055 remains proposed through preservation `fused-008`.

- The general Food `fused-006` curve loses its positive bend after restricting

Other predeclared settings do not establish a solution. Neighbor `x-y` without

too small is not proof of accurate admission.

The primary proposes 43 actually omitted notes for Food and 53 for Tools,

## Can any boundaries separate the saved useful and unwanted scores?

The user accepted the current omitted-note-focused query population rather than
expanding to every globally ungrouped entry, then authorized this diagnostic.
Keep the same 1,449 entries (1,426 from 281 wholly omitted eligible notes plus
23 controls), Food and Tools only. This is a mathematical capacity check of
the existing score, **not training, a deployed cutoff, an admission experiment
on unseen entries or proof of semantic accuracy**. No new vectors or LLM calls.

Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/reference-score-separation-v1/` in the retained
`oracle-mvp-hierarchy-food-halves-v1` worktree. The plan precedes capacity
outcomes; SHA256
`eb59d77c6889f5eb2152939ef196d013ab36147a27a9c3493d1d922b547beda6`.
It binds all source scores, labels, group IDs, code and controls. The reusable
calculation is `separation_bounds` in `fixed_reference_bend.py`. Previous source
bytes are verified through Git commit `74c48059`; old artifacts remain intact.

The logic accounts for **any-group** admission, not only the single best score
or a pairwise ranking. If group `g` admits an entry when `score >= cutoff_g`,
then rejecting every supplied unwanted entry requires:

`cutoff_g > max(score(unwanted_entry, g))` for every group.

A useful entry is therefore recoverable exactly when its score exceeds that
maximum in at least one group. These conditions are simultaneously sufficient
for every recoverable entry. A tie is inseparable. A blocked entry has a
rejection scoring at least as high in every group; **different rejections may
block different groups**. It is unnecessary for one rejection to dominate the
entry everywhere. Missing reference support remains unavailable, not a label.

This is an optimistic bound: use all 15 Food and ten Tools groups, regardless of
whether any bend exists or the self-check passes. No bound is applied to the
unreviewed omitted population. Label-dependent maxima are evidence about what
cutoffs could achieve; they are not chosen as new inference parameters.

The three overlapping review panels pool into 30 plausible Food entries and
49 plausible Tools entries, with 36 and 18 provisional mismatches respectively.
There are no conflicting labels across panels. Qualified/unresolved entries
are excluded from these primary positive/negative sets. Preserve all roles:

- Human rejection controls are the three specific entries Nausea, Blood and
  JAIA Drake, plus all 14 eligible headings of the previously rejected Easter
  broadcast note. The latter is one note-level decision, not 14 independent
  human judgments.
- Gas facilities and Breath are additional agent-assessed suspects.
- The complete Food rejection set combines the above with the 36 provisional
  panel mismatches, deduplicating to 51 entries. These are **not 51 human gold
  rejections**. Other-root membership is never used as a rejection.
- Earlier accepted notes are tested through any eligible heading as a possible
  route. Their individual headings do not thereby become positive entry gold.
  Traditional activities L02986 remains low priority, not relabeled negative.

| Distinct useful entries and rejection controls | Recoverable with separate group cutoffs | Recoverable with one common root cutoff |
|---|---:|---:|
| 30 provisional Food fits; 17 human-decision rejection entries | 30/30 | 28/30 |
| Same Food fits; add Gas facilities and Breath | 30/30 | 23/30 |
| Same Food fits; all 51 reviewed rejection entries | 30/30 | 22/30 |
| 49 provisional Tools fits; 18 provisional mismatches | 41/49 | 26/49 |

All three human-accepted Food notes retain a possible route while rejecting
the human control set, both with and without the two added suspects. With the
suspects included, a single common root cutoff cannot preserve the pisanki
note; separate group cutoffs can. This is note-route feasibility, not approval
of every candidate heading. The 29 higher-priority Food entries also all remain
recoverable under the full 51-entry rejection set; no apparent improvement is
manufactured by dropping Traditional activities.

Food examples against **all 51** rejection controls:

| Useful entry | One group permitting a separation | Useful score | Highest rejection score in that group |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| Coffee L00228 | Food `fused-014` | 0.084107 | 0.046273, Blood |
| Coffee beans L00374 | Food `fused-013` | 0.047644 | 0.032004, Gas facilities |
| Steaks L00479 | Food `fused-006` | 0.056892 | 0.027034, Spaces in configuration text |

For every saved plausible Food entry, at least one such interval exists. The
smallest best-group margin is `0.000837`, for low-priority Traditional
activities; excluding it, the smallest is `0.007353` for Eggs L02984. These
are not floating-point ties. **The current score family is capable of matching
this finite Food control set; the tested reference-derived boundary rules have
not found those separations reliably.** This does not establish that the same
scores separate unknown future fits or that these label-dependent intervals
can be located from accepted references alone.

The eight blocked Tools entries under the frozen provisional labels are Tablet
computers L00473, Video games L01883/L02215, Factories L03455, Reviews L03939,
Drones L04173, Smartwatches L06024 and Civil engineering L06928. The groupwise
maximum rejection witnesses come from only six entries: Retrieval L01811,
Planning L03774, Analysts L04113, Sports content solutions L05323, Deadlines
L05586 and Staff appointments L05884. None is new human rejection gold.

For example, Retrieval scores above Tablet computers in **all ten groups**.
In the Computers group, their scores are `0.073533` and `0.040276`; in Software,
`0.046683` and `0.037168`. Rejecting Retrieval while retaining that Tablet
computers entry is impossible using any of these per-group cutoffs. Likewise,
Sports content solutions alone dominates Factories and Civil engineering in
every group. Other blocked cases require several rejection witnesses together.
The contradiction is conditional on the provisional labels: review those
rejection assumptions before concluding the score must change. It does not
turn the user's uncertain Tools cases into established failures.

`cohort-details.json` saves every useful/group score, rejection maximum, tied
maximizing rejection IDs and margin, plus every rejection's complete group
scores. `tools-blocking-witnesses.json` preserves all ten-group witnesses for
each blocked entry. No cutoff vector or new hierarchy membership is exported.
The verifier constructs only an ephemeral mathematical witness on the supplied
examples to check the sufficiency claim; it never predicts unseen entries.

Seven new tests first fail because the diagnostic does not exist, then pass
through the calculation used by this audit. Forty-seven combined boundary/
query/fusion/HDBSCAN tests pass. Independent verification recomputes all 36,225
saved query/group scores from the unchanged meaning vectors and checks 4,235
capacity cells across 13 overlapping cohorts. Maximum score discrepancy is
`5.56e-17`. Source hashes and hierarchy state remain unchanged. Work per cohort
is `O((P+N)*G)`; no extra graph or model work. Actual HR35 coverage remains
718 notes. Findings/code/tests land on local `main`; retain this worktree for
frozen vector, score and evidence paths.

The unwanted scores are not attributable to one high pair alone. Gas facilities

## Food and Arts: can another allowed bend reject Gas facilities?

The user authorized tracing the Food Gas facilities route and comparing it
with Audio technology, keeping both calculations unchanged. Evidence is in
`target/food-halves-v1/food-arts-gas-boundary-trace-v1/` in the retained worktree;
plan SHA256
`e4ded0500aa746db2743b9d09bf3599d6037158e43004c291e4df3930e5ac3c2`.
The plan records the already-known admission outcomes and precedes the Food
pair decomposition and enumeration of the existing bend choices. No new rule,
cutoff, vector, provider call or hierarchy membership is introduced. Gas
facilities remains a provisional unwanted control, not a new human judgment.

Food preservation `fused-008` has five accepted entries from four source notes:
three Food preservation entries, Food-preservation mixtures, and Food L06359.
The last defines food broadly, mentioning preserved century eggs as an example;
the example does not restrict the whole meaning to preserved foods. Its
cross-note support `0.047969` is the weakest and supplies the curve anchor.
The other four accepted-member supports range from `0.140030` to `0.315200`.

Gas facilities L06055 matches Food preservation L00131 at `0.072883`, L00646
at `0.046922` and L00727 at `0.084368`; the mixture entry matches at `0.026963`
and generic Food at `0.007562`. The exact five-entry mean is `0.047740`.
Most of its numerical support comes from the
preservation entries rather than generic Food. This does not establish which
words caused those vector matches.

The neighbor-context bend chooses Lunches L00021 (`0.022692`) followed by
Meals L00608 (`0.021902`), giving midpoint **`0.022297`**. Neither is a member
of the preservation group. As in Audio technology, a bend among weaker
surrounding accepted references becomes the group's admission boundary.

Enumerating the existing curve choices reveals a stronger restriction. A
qualifying `y-x` bend must have positive deviation exceeding the existing
`1e-12` floating-point tolerance; the anchor and final endpoint have zero
deviation. Each candidate boundary remains the midpoint to the next lower
reference score. Retain the original fit interval and note weighting.

| Group / neighbor context | Gas score | Current boundary | Strictest qualifying bend's boundary | Can any qualifying bend reject Gas? |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Food preservation | 0.047740 | 0.022297 | 0.023568 | No |
| Audio technology | 0.025862 | 0.019374 | 0.024825 | No |

This is enumeration of saved reference-derived candidates, not label-based
selection of a replacement cutoff. Every qualifying midpoint in each curve
is at or below Gas's score. Choosing an earlier qualifying bend would therefore
still admit it through both groups. Both neighbor models also pass their
whole-note member-recovery checks, so the existing hard gate does not block
either route.

The constraints differ in an informative way if the positive-deviation
requirement is hypothetically relaxed, without applying any new boundary:

- In Food's neighbor curve, even the largest midpoint anywhere in the fitted
  interval is `0.047323`, below Gas. The weakest member `0.047969` is followed
  by another accepted Food entry at `0.046677`; their midpoint already admits
  Gas `0.047740`. Reordering which fitted midpoint is chosen cannot reject it.
- In Audio technology's neighbor curve, the first midpoint is `0.034277`, above
  Gas. That endpoint has zero bend deviation and is unavailable to the current
  positive-bend selection. Here the positive-bend constraint is material.

Full-root context is a control, not an adopted rescue. Food's strictest
qualifying full-context bend gives `0.046901`, still below Gas; allowing
nonpositive endpoints introduces one midpoint above Gas (`0.047861`). Arts
full-context already has qualifying cutpoints above Gas and its actual
`0.028976` boundary blocks this particular route. Both full-context groups
fail the existing hard self-check. None of these route observations establishes
the correctness of other admissions or resolves whole-root admission by itself.

The finite score-capacity results remain consistent: arbitrary separate group
boundaries can separate the saved controls, but the current curve construction
and allowed midpoint choices cannot realize every such separation. The
following section records the subsequently authorized diagnostic across the
saved Food and Arts controls.

Saved artifacts retain all member contributions, complete Food curves and
every considered midpoint/deviation for both groups and contexts. Exact Food
query-score/anchor replay, direct midpoint enumeration, contribution sums and
source hashes pass. Existing numerical code and previously accepted meanings
remain unchanged. No new algorithm is adopted and no notes are added.

## Across Food and Arts: which boundary constraints block the saved cases?

The user authorized checking the anchor, bend direction and midpoint restrictions
across the saved Food and Arts controls before choosing another formula. This
is a **capacity diagnostic**, not a new admission algorithm, threshold search
applied to unknown entries, training run or hierarchy change. Tools remains
outside scope. Reuse every reference group, graph weight, vector, score and
source-note exclusion unchanged.

Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/food-arts-boundary-constraints-v1/` in the
retained worktree. Plan SHA256
`7a94824fa0f3b3cd8b0256ee46e234dfa78304c91cc4b0740b83096a26187878`
was frozen before this all-cohort diagnostic. It records that the earlier
two-group Gas facilities traces were already known. Reconstruct reference
geometry through the existing `derive_boundary` production calculation;
member-anchored curves replay the saved Food/Arts bases with zero score or
boundary error. No numerical production code is edited.

Keep reference contexts separate: either the full accepted pool of the same
root, or members plus their direct fixed fusion neighbors. The latter never
adds an outsider or changes an edge. Within each context, vary three constraints:

1. Fit's upper endpoint: the existing weakest-member anchor, or the existing
   unanchored upper-10% trim. Replacing the endpoint is a controlled alternative,
   **not necessarily a relaxation**; it can move the fit in either direction.
   Preserve the existing lower-10% trim in every case.
2. Candidate bends: positive `y-x`, positive `x-y`, either nonzero direction,
   or any fitted reference point including zero-deviation endpoints. The
   `1e-12` tolerance remains unchanged. These enumerate possible points, not
   a new rule for choosing the strongest or any other bend.
3. Boundary placement: the existing midpoint between a selected reference
   score and its next lower score, or a hypothetical point anywhere in that
   same gap. For upper/lower scores `u > l`, the relaxed interval is `(l,u]`.
   A safe threshold admitting positive `p` exists exactly when
   `min(u,p) > max(l, largest_rejection_score)`.

Labels remain unchanged. The primary Food cohort has 30 provisional fits and
51 distinct rejection controls mixing human decisions and agent assessments.
Two sensitivity cohorts use only the 17 human-decision rejection headings
(three entry decisions plus 14 headings of one rejected post), then those plus
the two agent-suspect Gas/Breath entries. Arts has 24 provisional fits and 20
provisional mismatches; its seven unresolved entries remain excluded. Do not
present these as 51 or 20 independently validated human judgments.

For each choice family and group, require rejecting every supplied mismatch.
An available group with **no safe choice** must admit at least one mismatch
if kept active. Report those groups explicitly; a good route elsewhere does
not cancel their unwanted admissions. Separately compute an optimistic recall
ceiling allowing such groups to abstain. This hypothetical abstention is not
current policy, does not remove a group and is not hidden inside an accuracy
claim. The following table uses that optimistic ceiling throughout.

| Candidate choices, retaining the lower-10% trim | Food fits potentially retained / 30 | Arts fits potentially retained / 24 |
|---|---:|---:|
| Neighbor context; member anchor; any positive `y-x` bend; midpoint | 26 | 16 |
| Same, allow any fitted point including endpoints | 26 | 17 |
| Same, allow any position in each gap | 27 | 19 |
| Same, replace member anchor with the old upper-10% trim | 28 | 20 |
| Same, use full accepted-root context | 29 | 21 |
| Unrestricted separate group thresholds: earlier score-only bound | 30 | 24 |

These are hindsight upper bounds, **not measured results of an automatic new
rule**. They ask what choices the reference geometry makes possible. A single
simultaneously safe cutoff per group realizes every counted route; it is not
a different cutoff chosen for each positive. Numerical witnesses stay in
verification memory and are not exported as learned/deployed cutoff vectors.

Specific findings:

- Reversing bend direction alone does not solve the problem. With neighbor
  context, member anchor and midpoint, the opposite `x-y` candidate family
  retains at most 13/30 Food and 3/24 Arts, versus 26/30 and 16/24 for `y-x`.
  Allowing both nonzero directions still gives 26/30 and 16/24. Permitting
  endpoints is a distinct relaxation from merely changing bend polarity.
- Under current neighbor choices, Food has two active groups with no safe
  threshold against the full rejection set, and Arts has one. Permitting any
  fitted point removes Arts' unsafe group, but Food preservation remains unsafe
  until placement inside a gap is also allowed. Neither modification chooses
  a replacement cutoff automatically.
- Free placement restores a possible route for Eggs L02984 in Food and Film
  editions L03394 plus editorial Cartoons L05979 in Arts. Their score orderings
  already permit separation; a forced midpoint blocks a viable interval.
- The member anchor can leave too few fitted points. Neighbor-context Artists
  `fused-010`, for example, remains unavailable regardless of bend direction
  or midpoint/free placement. Replacing the anchor changes that availability
  and can restore a route for Art galleries; it does not certify the route.
- Neighbor context itself can exclude useful boundary values. Coffee beans
  L00374 scores `0.047644` against Food `fused-013`, above its largest full-panel
  mismatch score `0.032004`. But the lowest adjacent reference score available
  to its fitted neighbor gaps is `0.069263`; even that context's complete raw
  curve bottoms at `0.063347`. Other potential Coffee-bean routes are also
  blocked by the restricted fit. Full accepted-root context supplies enough
  lower-valued references for a possible route. This does not add other
  outsiders to the graph or establish full-context automatic accuracy.

### The remaining Arts misses expose the lower trim

Even with full accepted-root context, no bend-direction restriction and free
gap placement, the preserved lower-10% trim blocks three plausible Arts entries:
Avatar films L03389, The Lord of the Rings films L03393 and JAIA Drake L04019.
The problem is visible in specific feasible-score intervals:

| Outside entry / mathematical score route | Entry score | Largest mismatch score in that group | Lowest adjacent score allowed by the fitted gaps |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Avatar films / Artists `fused-010` | 0.014101 | 0.011340 | 0.014922 |
| Lord of the Rings films / Artists `fused-010` | 0.012311 | 0.011340 | 0.014922 |
| JAIA Drake / Songs `fused-012` | 0.015072 | 0.011932 | 0.017459 |

Each positive exceeds all supplied rejection controls through at least one
group, but it is below every boundary available in that group's fitted region.
The full accepted-reference curves do contain lower values (`0.002870` for
Artists and `0.007075` for Songs). Those lower reference levels have been
trimmed out. These are mathematical root-admission routes, not semantic
certification of an edge from a particular reference group to the outsider.

The analogous remaining Food miss under full context is Traditional activities
L02986, which the user already marked low priority. Its score `0.023273` clears
the relevant rejection maximum `0.022436`, but the fitted gap floor is
`0.025139`; the complete curve extends down to `0.010434`. Do not turn this
case into the main optimization target or infer that the user rejected it.

The human-rejection Food sensitivities do not erase the main limitation.
Neighbor/current choices retain at most 28/30 with human rejections only, then
26/30 when Gas/Breath suspects are included. With full-root context, no bend
restriction and free gaps, all 30 are mathematically recoverable against either
of those two rejection sets. The additional full-panel mismatch labels alter
other available groups, so sensitivity and label authority remain material.

This isolates a concrete next test: remove the lower-tail exclusion and compare
the automatic calculation on Food and Arts with everything else held fixed.
The following section records that subsequently authorized automatic test.
Finding a feasible range with supplied labels does not tell the algorithm how
to identify it from references alone.

The audit covers 128 cohort/constraint/context combinations, with 62,400
independent scalar candidate checks and 1,852 simultaneous safe-choice witness
checks. Midpoint-to-free-gap and direction/endpoints relaxations satisfy
monotonic recoverability checks. Saved source and graph hashes remain unchanged;
no unknown-query predictions or chosen cutoff vectors are produced. Calculation
takes about five seconds locally. No model calls, new vectors, hierarchy/UI
changes or admission-policy adoption.

## Removing lower-tail trimming: paired automatic test

The user authorized removing only the lower-10% exclusion in an automatic test
on Food and Arts. This section reports actual automatic proposals, **not** the
preceding hindsight capacity bounds. The ablation does not qualify a new rule
and is not adopted as the default.

Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/lower-tail-ablation-v1/` in the retained
worktree. Plan SHA256
`00b7bd389012378b088d303f5be94aac942b1f95cc5de510504deadfa4bddcca`
was frozen after the production-path tests passed and before new admission
outcomes. It binds historical source bytes from `d54a966b`, current experimental
source/tests, original reference models/predictions, vectors, groups and graphs.

Minimal reusable support adds `trim_lower=True` to `derive_boundary` and
`qualify_boundary` in `fixed_reference_bend.py`. Default results retain their
original structure and numerical values. The explicit experimental
`trim_lower=False` keeps the entire lower tail and records
`lower_trim_levels=0`; it does not alter the member anchor or upper endpoint.
Forward the option through the base and every whole-note fold. No generator,
prompt, runner or existing hierarchy configuration is changed.

Keep the same groups (15 Food, 25 Arts), source-note exclusions, meaning-kernel
score, 1,449 queries, source-note-weighted reference curves, member anchor,
`y-x` bend orientation, strongest-bend selection and adjacent-score midpoint.
Paired contexts remain the full accepted pool of the same root versus direct
fixed fusion neighbors. Removing the lower tail exclusion changes the fitted
lower endpoint, so normalized coordinates and the chosen bend can change;
that is a consequence of the single intervention, not extra parameter tuning.

Primary ungated results use neighbor context, as in the latest prior test.
Retain the previously frozen provisional labels and human decisions separately.

| Root / context, ungated | Plausible before → after | Rejection controls before → after | Proposed omitted notes before → after |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Food / neighbors | 21/30 → 24/30 | 3/51 → 5/51 | 43 → 47 |
| Arts / neighbors | 17/24 → 17/24 | 3/20 → 3/20 | 174 → 174 |
| Food / full accepted root | 27/30 → 29/30 | 2/51 → 20/51 | 34 → 144 |
| Arts / full accepted root | 17/24 → 24/24 | 3/20 → 14/20 | 171 → 279 |

These counts are proposals, not added notes, independent test accuracy or new
human gold. There are 281 eligible entirely omitted notes in this fixed query
population. Arts' full-context proposal of 279 is an especially broad outcome,
not a coverage success established by its 24/24 plausible selections.

The Food neighbor variant gains Steaks L00479, Seed oils L00627 and Charity
dinners L02693 without losing a provisional fit. It also newly selects Economy
L03778 and the agent-suspect Breath L04399. Its three explicit human entry
rejections remain excluded, and all four prior human note decisions still
match. The preserved Food group's Gas route is not repaired.

The mechanism is concrete in the neighbor models:

- General Food `fused-006` changes from no positive bend to boundary `0.031604`;
  it gains a usable ungated route but still fails its whole-note self-check.
- Eating `fused-010` changes from insufficient levels to boundary `0.046225`,

- Food preservation `fused-008` stays at `0.022297`, so Gas facilities remains

- Arts' Artists `fused-010` gains enough fitted levels but has no positive bend,

  panel therefore does not improve.

Full-context results expose the difference between enabling feasible ranges
and actually choosing useful boundaries. Arts' Artists `fused-010` boundary
falls **`0.033753 → 0.005758`** and passes its self-check. This recovers the
previously missed films, creator, exhibition and cartoons while also admitting
many unrelated cases. Its all-group result includes Milk, Bread, Steaks, Water,
Savings schemes and Gas facilities among the provisional mismatches. The
automatic strongest-bend choice does not isolate the feasible separating
intervals established by the preceding mathematical audit.

Food's full-context result also newly admits the previously human-rejected

human entry rejections Nausea, Blood and JAIA Drake remain excluded. Traditional
activities becomes available on the already accepted pisanki note, but the
user marked that heading low priority and its addition does not offset the
unwanted broadcast admission.

The hard self-check remains a secondary diagnostic, not a remedy to adopt:
Food-neighbor gated results stay 17/30 and 1/51; Food-full gated results decline
14/30 to 12/30 while staying 1/51. Arts-neighbor gated results stay 17/24 and
3/20; Arts-full gated results become 24/24 and 14/20, the same overbroad result
as ungated. Retaining accepted members does not establish rejection quality.

Four new tests first fail on the unsupported `trim_lower` keyword, then pass
through production functions. They verify restored lower levels without moving

All 51 combined boundary/query/fusion/HDBSCAN tests pass.

Default replay is exact for 80 base models, 430 whole-note folds and 11,592
