## HR36 completed test of the unchanged automatic rules

The human approved local meaning encoding and testing the unchanged automatic
admission rules. Evidence:
`target/food-halves-v1/hr36-meaning-transfer-v1/` in the retained task worktree.
Frozen plan SHA256
`f2de3ad7900184363db63cb5bd4376084cf337c2cf3451c079e18e6ea56d9ca9`.
This is a completed numerical probe, **not an activated hierarchy policy**.
There are no LLM calls, training, paid resources, prompt edits or actual
membership changes. Generated reports, vectors and source-bound numerical
scripts remain ignored; the existing production calculations are unchanged.

### Transfer protocol and independent review

Score all **1,121 eligible omitted entries from 254 HR36 notes** independently.
The remaining 63 omitted notes have no usable meaning. Reuse the exact old
Food/Arts references, fusion groups and thresholds. Separately apply the same
recipe to each HR36 root's initial accepted entries; later amendments,
descendants and outsiders never enlarge those reference sets. Synthetic titles
are not score inputs or semantic gold boundaries.

Freeze the rules before HR36 outcomes: mean meaning-kernel affinity within each
fixed group; accept when any group passes its unchanged automatic boundary.
The primary boundary is the exact weakest existing cross-note member mean.
The control is the earlier member-anchored bend over fixed fusion neighbors,
`orientation=y_minus_x`, with the original lower trim. Neither uses the
all-fold gate, fits labels or inserts queries into the accepted graphs.

Before scoring, freeze a review panel: one hashed entry from each of 40 hashed
omitted notes plus 48 title-focused entries, deduplicated to **85 entries from
62 notes**. Inspect complete meanings/posts and record provisional Food/Arts
judgments separately from predictions. Review-input SHA256
`7793e154ab046aa98eb8596e6668e0a31fa868484c17d9f19f91dd7fd14bd74c`;
assessment SHA256
`48b47a2c2e4aff62d11693d6277388a47c70d221e51c8ddb5e4d3373b833895f`.
These are **agent assessments, not human gold**. The panel is small and partly
focused; it does not establish population accuracy or author-independent
generalization. Unresolved entries stay unresolved.

### The old roots do not transfer uniformly

These are actual unchanged-rule proposals, not the earlier 57/63 hindsight
capacity bound. Each root has its own panel judgments.

| Fixed accepted references | Automatic boundary | Plausible entries selected | Unsupported entries selected | Unresolved selected |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| HR35 Food | Weakest member | 15/19 | 1/62 | 1/4 |
| HR35 Food | Existing anchored neighbor bend | 19/19 | 5/62 | 1/4 |
| HR35 Arts | Weakest member | 5/18 | 1/57 | 0/10 |
| HR35 Arts | Existing anchored neighbor bend | 8/18 | 18/57 | 5/10 |

Food's four missed plausible entries are one Food definition and three Coffee
definitions. Its clear unwanted panel match is Nights, whose meaning is the
period between sunset and sunrise and whose post describes sleeping on a floor.
Arts misses Books, languages, some films and film-related activities, and selects
generic Software performance through the Audio group. Moving to the old bend
does not provide a general repair: it adds many technical/nonmedia matches.

Reference coverage also matters. Twenty-six accepted Food entries, including
many generic Food headings, remain outside the 15 fixed fusion groups; they
provide curve context but no independent passing group. Thirty accepted Arts
entries likewise remain outside its groups, mostly Music/Songs/Voices. The Arts
references overall are music-heavy and contain no dedicated book or language
group. The frozen broader Arts expectations are retained, but these misses
also test expansion beyond observed topic families. They do not prove that
threshold choice alone explains all of the transfer gap.

The whole-note objective matters. Considering every query heading from notes
that have a pre-reviewed plausible entry, weakest-member Food recovers
**8/10 notes**, Arts **6/14**. The six Arts routes concern television series,
films, film history, cinema enjoyment/Netflix, and Pixar/acting. These extra
routes were inspected after prediction; a missed heading does not automatically
mean its whole post was missed. Conversely, a good post does not validate every
heading selected from it.

Reviewing **all 39 weakest-member Food proposals** after selection gives
30 plausible entries, seven unsupported and two unresolved, across 16 notes.
Eleven notes plausibly fit Food/dining and five do not: heritage wallpaper
preservation, generic travel, a crime-witness reward, nighttime hardship and
a celebrity return visit. Cruise-line identity and general Cruise news are
also weak/association-based entry matches, but their pizza post has legitimate
Pizzerias/Culinary arts matches. The Tim Hortons case is dining-venue evidence,
not a meal description; retain that qualification. This complete proposal audit
is distinct from the pre-score control panel and is still provisional.

### Applying the same recipe to HR36's own roots

Keep the existing fusion settings: 20 neighbors, sigma 0.5, 20 iterations,
HDBSCAN min_samples 3 / min_cluster_size 2 / EOM. Use only each root's original
accepted entries. This changes the references to the new corpus; it does not
change a rule in response to its outcomes.

| HR36 root | Initial reference entries | Fixed fusion groups | Weakest-member proposed entries | Proposed omitted notes |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| C00001 · Beings and society | 904 | 102 | 702 | 233 |
| C00002 · Experience and expression | 104 | 13 | 11 | 11 |
| C00003 · Society and security | 120 | 16 | 39 | 28 |
| C00004 · Activities and events | 170 | 20 | 48 | 39 |
| C00005 · Activities and events | 46 | 4 | 77 | 51 |
| C00006 · Artificial intelligence | 46 | 2 | 5 | 5 |

Deduplicated union: **736 entries from 239 omitted notes**. This is a proposal
count, not 239 validated recoveries. The control bend proposes 547 entries from
215 distinct omitted notes. No graph receives any of them.

As specified before scoring, audit up to 12 primary proposals per root, ordered
by the fixed hash rather than score magnitude. The resulting **64 entries from
59 notes** have 36 plausible, 14 unsupported and 14 unresolved root matches.
This is a separate post-selection audit; root/sample observations are not
independent. Root fit is assessed against the accepted topic composition,
not just the synthetic name or the particular passing group. For example,
Books can cohere with Articles/Digital content even when the numerical passing
route is Photography. Ambiguous expansions remain qualified.

### Concrete mechanism: one weak reference controls the whole boundary

The strongest evidence against adopting the rule unchanged is reproducible
from the accepted-member scores, without demanding perfect semantic grading:

| HR36 group | Weakest member that sets the threshold | Threshold | Median member score | Outside entries passing this group alone |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| C00001 · Technology | L01694 · Technology, defined as the **named posting destination** for a mango-farming post | 0.01012 | 0.29153 | 463 |
| C00001 · Photography | L04473 · IRIB, Iran's state broadcasting organization | 0.00685 | 0.19891 | 549 |

These proposal sets overlap and cannot be added. The first group accounts for
194 omitted notes, the second 217. Ordinary food, diets, sexual activity and
other unrelated topics pass these low boundaries. The named Technology
destination is a preserved meaning, not silently reinterpreted as the field.
Fusion has grouped it with technology headings, and the minimum-member rule
allows that weak member to set the threshold for every future query.

Other clear concerns survive outside those two groups: C00005 gaming/sports
proposes generic Servers, Operating systems, open-source licensing and form
handling. C00006 proposes military Intelligence operations and Random selection
along with the plausible LLM/Chatbot cases; general Predictive models remain
unresolved. A large proposal count is therefore insufficient evidence of useful
coverage. Neither this diagnosis nor the audits establish that the boundary is
the only problem: reference composition, score orderings and broad meanings
remain relevant.

**Do not adopt the weakest-member rule unchanged as a universal admission
policy.** This is compatible with accepting practical imperfection: the exposed
cross-root instability is larger than a few disputed cases. The next useful
numerical test is a boundary derived from the main body of accepted-member
support that cannot be controlled by a single weak member. Preserve these
groups, scores and reviewed cases; do not introduce a global similarity cutoff,
new LLM judgments or training. That replacement is suggested only, not run here.

### Native encoding, verification and disposition

Encode 2,469 new exact contexts and nine replay contexts using the unchanged
native SSE worker, checkpoint and tokenizer on local CPU with 12 threads.
Each context yields both title- and full-meaning pooled vectors in one encoder
pass: **4,956 rows**. Thirty-eight required entries reuse 33 exact prior
contexts. The longest new input is 124 tokens; no truncation. The actual runtime
is Torch 2.13.0+cpu / Transformers 4.57.1 / Tokenizers 0.22.2, recorded in the
new immutable receipt; its container label is historical identity, not rented
compute.

Nine exact context replays / 18 vector rows have maximum absolute error
`4.47e-8` and cosine error `8.09e-14`. All 2,511 HR36 native-title comparisons
pass, maximum cosine error `2.17e-13`. Validate text spans, token selections,
receipt identities, raw vector hashes, checkpoint/base artifact and all frozen
source hashes. The numerical evaluation produces 220,837 query/group scores;
2,561 independent scalar group means differ by at most `2.78e-17`, independent
kernel error at most `8.33e-16`. Isolated/reordered/duplicate queries preserve
scores, and accepted graph bytes remain unchanged.

An initial exact-equality threshold replay check differed by `6.66e-16` through
floating-point arithmetic. Preserve its script/log; use the existing scalar
arithmetic tolerance `1e-14` for that verification. **The old threshold values
and admission comparisons remain unchanged.** The first preparation attempt
was also interrupted to avoid repeatedly hashing the same state per input;
the state hash is read once before freezing the plan. Neither issue made an
extra encoding call or changed a teacher receipt.

All 62 relevant existing production-path numerical tests pass. Evaluation
takes 6.32 seconds after encoding. Keep `plan.json`, native request/config,
`preflight.json`, immutable `worker-result/`, vector bindings, saved full-view
matrices, all query/group scores and boundary models, panel/audit files and
`verification.json`. `review.md` is the compact report. Maintained findings
land on local `main`; retain the worktree for required frozen source/vector
paths. No hierarchy UI refresh or official hierarchy-run/spreadsheet change.

## Member-only distribution boundary v1: excessive useful-match loss

The human approved the proposed test of a boundary derived from existing
members' score distribution rather than its minimum. Keep all reference
groups, query similarity scores, meanings and previous judgments fixed.
Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/member-distribution-boundary-v1/` in the
retained task worktree. Frozen plan SHA256
`f3b7df085c891fa26dbbe9ea27fd56266b8df274ba825709b84e256c6ff21a5b`.
The method and implementation were frozen before its changed admission
outcomes. **This hypothesis fails the useful-admission objective and is not
adopted.** Landing experimental code does not activate it in HiGen.

### One reference-only hypothesis

The new experimental `fixed_reference_member_distribution.derive_boundary`
consumes the existing cross-note member support scores and source-note IDs.
Sort distinct scores strongest first and merge equal levels. Each source note
contributes one total unit of cumulative curve mass across its entries.
Normalize cumulative mass to x and the drop in **log support** to y. Select
the largest positive interior x-y deviation, then use the geometric midpoint
between that score and the next lower score as the admission boundary.

Log support expresses multiplicative differences in the existing exponential
kernel; this is one frozen hypothesis, not a raw/log parameter search. There
is no trimming, fitted mixture, label fitting, outsider participation or global
similarity cutoff. Fewer than three distinct levels or no positive bend retain
the old minimum. Unavailable cross-note support or zero support cannot invent
a logarithm/boundary. The existing 1e-12 collinearity tolerance suppresses
numerical bends; a representably zero log span retains the minimum.

All original members remain in the group and in the unchanged query mean.
This changes only the admission boundary. It can only tighten the previous
minimum, so every new proposal must be a subset of the preceding proposals.
The calculation costs O(m log m) for m members, O(m) storage; all query/group
scores are reused. No new encodings, query kernels or outside/outside graph.

### Fewer unwanted matches, but too many useful ones disappear

Replay the existing HR35 Food/Arts regression queries and all 1,121 HR36
queries against the two old and six new reference sets. Do not relabel any
case. The panels below overlap, are development evidence and mostly contain
provisional agent judgments; do not pool them as independent accuracy samples.

| Existing panel | Plausible selected: minimum → distribution bend | Unsupported selected: minimum → distribution bend |
|---|---:|---:|
| HR35 Food, 30 plausible / 51 unsupported controls | 14 → 5 | 3 → 0 |
| HR35 expanded Arts, 63 plausible / 30 unsupported | 54 → 20 | 10 → 1 |
| HR36 Food pre-score panel, 19 plausible / 62 unsupported | 15 → 10 | 1 → 0 |
| HR36 Arts pre-score panel, 18 plausible / 57 unsupported | 5 → 2 | 1 → 0 |
| Prior selected sample across six HR36 roots | 36 → 9 | 14 → 0 |

In the six-root sample, unresolved retained cases also fall from 14 to two;
they remain unresolved, not reclassified as mistakes. The three human-reviewed
Food entry rejections cannot become new positives under this subset rule.
The **human note-level controls** reveal actual regressions: culinary Eggs
(`L00101`'s note) and the accepted Easter-egg note (`L02984`) lose every route.
Egg yolks retain the `L00438` route, and the rejected Easter broadcast remains
rejected. Keep all previous user decisions intact.

The complete preceding HR36 Food proposal audit permits checking its entire
new subset: plausible selected headings 30 → 17, unsupported seven → zero,
unresolved two → zero; plausible notes 11 → eight, unsupported notes five →
zero. The separate pre-score useful-note retrieval panel changes **8/10 →
7/10 for Food**, **6/14 → 3/14 for Arts**. These are different cohorts and
units. Do not use the cleaner eight-note selection to conceal lost useful
coverage. Across HR36's own roots, mechanical proposals fall from 736 entries
/ 239 notes to 130 entries / 84 notes; that is not 84 validated recoveries.

### The failure is visible in the accepted-member distributions

| HR36 group | Minimum boundary | New boundary | Queries passing this group alone: old → new |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| C00001 Technology | 0.01012 | 0.03593 | 463 → 71 |
| C00001 Photography | 0.00685 | 0.11836 | 549 → 2 |
| C00005 Sports | 0.04857 | 0.16463 | 29 → 2 |
| C00006 AI models/agents | 0.10813 | 0.17511 | 5 → 0 |

Technology does isolate its single weak named-posting reference. But the same
rule changes **176 of 197 groups**, including ordinary groups. Twelve resulting
boundaries exclude a majority of their own existing entries. A positive bend
partitions score variation; it does not establish that the lower part consists
of exceptional bad references.

The Sports example exposes a more basic assumption. Existing members have
median cross-note support **0.22743**, while the legitimate new subtopics score
**0.05502 for Association football, 0.05861 for Cricket and 0.07092 for
Basketball**. Those are above the old 0.04857 boundary but below the new
0.16463 boundary. Many reference meanings repeat the broad heading, whereas
the new meanings describe narrower topics. Strong agreement among existing
definitions therefore does not by itself provide an admission radius for
valid new subtopics. The AI change similarly removes LLM and chatbot proposals
along with the unwanted military-intelligence/random-selection cases.

**Reject this distribution-bend hypothesis as a general replacement.** It
reduces unwanted admissions by closing too much useful expansion. This does
not establish that all reference-only rules are impossible, nor that similarity
scores alone explain every failure. The next useful investigation is whether
the saved title and meaning connections can distinguish isolated weak reference
matches from legitimate narrower-topic variation. Inspect the named Technology
destination, IRIB and ordinary Sports controls before choosing another boundary.
That next investigation is suggested only and is not started here.

### Verification, reproducibility and integration

Write tests against the new numerical function first and observe the expected
missing-module failure. A separate near-identical-support test reproduces
division by zero when distinct floats have identical represented logarithms;
handling that zero span makes it pass without inventing an epsilon support.
Eight new production-path tests plus 62 relevant existing regressions pass:
isolated weak member, equal/small levels, log-linear scores, tied levels and
equal note mass, permutation, unavailable/zero support and invalid input.
The evaluator calls this same function; no testing-only production API or
runner/constructor seam is added.

The replay checks **197 boundaries and 278,797 saved query/group cells** across
the two corpora. Every old group and root prediction exactly replays. All new
proposals are subsets of old ones. Independent geometric-midpoint arithmetic
differs by at most `1.11e-16`; permutation and source-hash checks pass. Query
scores, reference memberships, all reviewed labels and frozen producer outputs
remain unchanged. Evaluation takes **1.18 seconds**. There are no LLM calls,
encodings, training, paid resources, hierarchy/prompt/UI changes or activation.

`implementation.json` pins the new function and tests before evaluation;
`evaluation-source.json` pins the data-specific evaluation script. Retain full
reference curves/models, old/new query predictions, cohort and human-control
results, complete Food-audit subset results, six-root sample results, numerical
diagnostics and `verification.json`. `review.md` is the compact report. Land
the reusable experimental module, tests and maintained findings on local `main`;
keep the task worktree for these frozen artifact/vector paths. The previous
minimum remains the comparison control, not an approved universal policy.

## HR36 title/meaning disagreement: explanatory, not a general boundary rule

The human approved inspecting the saved title and meaning connections for
Technology, IRIB and Sports controls before proposing another boundary.
Keep the reference memberships, meanings, fusion settings, outsider scores and
all previous judgments fixed. This diagnostic makes **no admission change**.
Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/reference-view-disagreement-v1/` in the
retained food-halves worktree. The measure/case plan was frozen before
calculation, SHA256
`07342c7dee6ca01df774acc2479df54dae9dbedbb930425f1f64aaf32bbbd94f`.

### Two different weak-reference mechanisms

Reconstruct the two accepted-only local graphs from the existing saved vectors,
using the unchanged mutual top-20 / sigma 0.5 calculation. Title pooling selects
title tokens with the meaning in context; meaning pooling selects the full
meaning with the title in context. Neither is an original-post mask vector.
Exclude the member's whole source note when measuring its group support.

Connections in this table are cross-note edges inside the assigned group in
each local graph. Mean cosines include every available group peer, whether or
not a graph edge exists. These quantities are not interchangeable.

| Entry | Title / meaning connections | Mean title cosine | Mean meaning cosine |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| L01694 Technology: named posting destination | 18 / 0 | 0.9663 | 0.1585 |
| L04473 IRIB: Iranian broadcaster | 1 / 0 | 0.1133 | 0.0913 |
| L05361 Imago: photography agency | 1 / 4 | 0.1720 | 0.4190 |
| L00173 Photographers | 15 / 7 | 0.7315 | 0.4272 |
| L04647 Content exclusion from the sports front | 12 / 2 | 0.6044 | 0.4237 |
| L03069 Sports coverage | 13 / 11 | 0.8416 | 0.6251 |
| L04632 AI agents: language-model-based autonomous software | 3 / 0 | 0.9255 | 0.6315 |

**Technology exhibits strong title / weak meaning support.** L01694's meaning
identifies a named posting destination. Its four meaning-graph connections
outside the group all lead to Memestr posting destinations. Its title instead
connects to every other Technology-group member. This is concrete evidence
that the two views are connecting different senses.

**IRIB is weak in both views.** Its sole title connection is to Imago, which
has four meaning connections to photographers, cameras and photos. IRIB has
no off-diagonal meaning edges anywhere in this root. The existing final fusion
nevertheless connects IRIB to all 17 other group members, strongest to Imago.
That is consistent with the exchange propagating this bridge; the inspection
is not an ablation establishing exclusive causality.

The same missing-edge symptom also occurs for **a legitimate AI-agent
meaning**: L04632 explicitly describes language-model-based autonomous agents,
yet has no meaning edges anywhere in its accepted root. Its three title edges
lead to other AI-agent definitions, and final fusion connects it to 14 group
peers. Mean meaning cosine is still 0.6315. Mutual-neighbor pruning does not
make zero edges a semantic rejection certificate.

Across all 951 group memberships in the three inspected roots / 108 groups,
**51 have some in-group title edges but no in-group meaning edges**. These
include ordinary Bitcoin and AI definitions. Do not promote that symptom into
an automatic filter. The exploratory title-to-meaning kernel-support ratio
also fails to identify both focal problems consistently: Technology is 82.73
versus the next Technology member's 6.91, but IRIB is only 3.72, below the
valid Photographers meaning's 7.50. No ratio cutoff is fitted.

### Narrower outside topics can legitimately have weak title support

Score the 15 planned outsiders independently against their fixed groups; never
insert an outsider into either graph. The following are the unchanged mean
`exp(-5.5*(1-cosine))` supports, not edge weights or probabilities.

| Outside entry | Title support | Meaning support | Existing meaning minimum |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Association football | 0.08619 | 0.05502 | 0.04857 |
| Cricket | 0.04130 | 0.05861 | 0.04857 |
| Basketball, L02063 | 0.05866 | 0.07092 | 0.04857 |
| Large language models | 0.01986 | 0.14070 | 0.10813 |
| Chatbots | 0.03632 | 0.16695 | 0.10813 |

All five planned Sports outsiders and both LLM/chatbot controls score below
every accepted member on the title view, while passing the existing meaning
minimum. Requiring title support to exceed even the weakest existing member
would lose these group routes. This is a focal-group comparison, not a claim
that every corresponding note loses all possible root routes.

**Disposition:** the diagnostic explains why weak references can enter the
fixed groups, but does not establish a generally reliable way to remove them
from boundary estimation. Technology and IRIB require different explanations;
ordinary subtopics and valid sparsely connected members overlap the numerical
symptoms. Keep meaning-only outsider scoring unchanged. Do not adopt a
both-views requirement, zero-edge exclusion or new cutoff from this evidence.
The original minimum and rejected distribution bend remain experimental
controls; this investigation has not resolved the general admission boundary.
No new human gold labels or semantic accuracy estimates are created.

### Verification and saved evidence

All 951 existing meaning-support values replay within `1.67e-16`. Independent
scalar calculations check 3,348 view/pair values, maximum error `3.67e-15`.
Graph symmetry, positive weights and the top-20 degree bound pass. All frozen
source hashes remain unchanged. Core calculation takes **0.74 seconds**.
No production code changes, so no new testing seam or runtime part is added.

Save all member values, complete focal pair scores and per-view edges,
independent outside controls and their nearest matches, and the reconstructed
per-view graphs/cosines. The additional existing-final-fusion inspection and
derived support ratios are explicitly exploratory, with their source hashes
in `exploratory-summary.json`. `evaluation-sources.json` pins the ignored
calculation scripts; `verification.json` and `review.md` retain checks and the
compact report. This is a small-reference diagnostic, not qualification of an
all-pairs production graph at larger corpus sizes.

There are no provider calls, new encodings, training, paid resources, prompt
changes, hierarchy changes or UI updates. Land these maintained findings on
local `main`; retain the task worktree for frozen evidence/vector paths.

## HR36 score capacity: promising bounds, no automatic admission rule

The human approved comparing saved HR36 meaning scores for already-reviewed
useful and unwanted entries, before another boundary experiment. Preserve all
judgments, unresolved cases, memberships, scores and previous predictions.
Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/hr36-score-capacity-v1/` in the retained
food-halves worktree. Frozen measure/case plan SHA256
`1ea6336c048fcd2716a7ea5cfe4bdda435d9fbee69ac12ad5bf83255038cf27a`.
**Every bound below uses the supplied labels in hindsight. None is an automatic
result, training run, deployed cutoff or unseen-performance estimate.**

### Exact diagnostic and separate review panels

Use the existing production `fixed_reference_bend.separation_bounds` function.
An entry passes when any fixed group's mean meaning score reaches that group's
boundary. To reject all reviewed unwanted entries, a group boundary must be
strictly above its largest unwanted score. A useful entry can therefore survive
exactly when it exceeds that maximum in at least one group. Different unwanted
entries can block different groups. This is a mathematical bound for the
supplied cases, not a prescription for choosing boundaries without labels.

Only groups with an existing finite weakest-member boundary participate; all
212 group instances across these nine panels are available. The repeated Food
panels are separate and overlapping, not independent groups or accuracy samples.
Compare unrestricted separate group boundaries with **tightening only**: no
boundary may fall below the existing minimum. Tightening measures preservation
of already selected useful entries; unrestricted bounds can also expose useful
entries currently rejected by excessively high boundaries. Unresolved judgments
never constrain either bound and remain separately recorded.

### HR36 queries against old Food/Arts references, judged before scores

| Existing reference set | Useful / unwanted / unresolved controls | Existing useful / unwanted admitted | Best possible useful, rejecting all unwanted | Same bound, tightening only |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| HR35 Food | 19 / 62 / 4 | 15 / 1 | 19/19 | 15/19 |
| HR35 Arts | 18 / 57 / 10 | 5 / 1 | 15/18 | 5/18 |

Thus the scores permit retaining all Food controls and most Arts controls while
rejecting all reviewed unwanted examples. But recovering the four additional
Food and ten additional Arts entries requires lowering some boundaries.
Tightening alone cannot expand coverage beyond the old predictions. These
panels mix random-note and title-focused sampling and provisional agent labels;
they are not representative accuracy estimates or human gold.

### HR36 own-root sample, reviewed after selection

| Root | Useful / unwanted / unresolved controls | Best possible useful retained, rejecting all unwanted | Boundaries that must rise in this sample |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| C00001 | 6 / 5 / 1 | 6/6 | 2/102 |
| C00002 | 8 / 0 / 3 | Unconstrained: no unwanted controls | 0/13 |
| C00003 | 9 / 1 / 2 | 9/9 | 1/16 |
| C00004 | 6 / 1 / 5 | 6/6 | 1/20 |
| C00005 | 5 / 5 / 2 | 5/5 | 1/4 |
| C00006 | 2 / 2 / 1 | 2/2 | 1/2 |

Across the **five roots with unwanted controls**, separate boundaries could
retain all **28 useful entries** and reject all **14 unwanted entries**, even
using tightening only. Exactly six group boundaries must rise in the
label-dependent construction. The other eight useful cases have no unwanted
controls, so do not include them in a claimed rejection test. All 64 examples
were previously selected; this sample cannot measure missed useful entries.

The feasible Sports score ranges retain association football, cricket,
basketball and player suspensions while excluding the five reviewed generic
software/servers cases. AI ranges retain LLMs and chatbots while excluding
military intelligence and random selection. Minimum best usable-group margins
above unwanted scores are 0.04330 for these Sports cases and 0.01628 for AI.
C00001's narrowest useful route is Books: 0.01784 versus Food's 0.01026 in the
Photography group, a margin of 0.00758. These are kernel-score differences, not
probabilities or guarantees for the rest of the corpus.

### Imperfect tradeoffs and inseparable cases

Exhaustively enumerate permitting up to zero, one or two reviewed unwanted
entries. Every unwanted entry outside the permitted subset still constrains
all groups. Maximize retained useful cases, preferring fewer actual unwanted
admissions for ties, then stable lexicographic enumeration. This diagnoses
imperfection without choosing a production error budget or exporting a rule.

Allowing one or two unwanted cases improves none of the preceding bounds.
The complete post-selection Food audit is the exception: unrestricted boundaries
can retain all 30 plausible headings and reject all seven unwanted headings;
tightening alone preserves **29/30**. The lost route is Tim Hortons. Allowing
one unwanted entry restores 30/30 under tightening. Pizzerias survives with
only a **0.000432** margin above an unwanted control. Keep its pizza note's
valid route distinct from suspect headings such as cruise news; entry and
whole-note errors remain different questions. Do not pool this selected audit
with the pre-score panel or claim representative note coverage.

Three pre-score Arts headings cannot exceed all unwanted controls in any of
its 25 fixed groups: **L00764 Requests for information, L02605 Fabrizio Romano,
and L03598 English language**. Their closest-to-separable route is Songs, where
Software inputs scores higher; other unwanted controls block the other groups.
Their maximum margins are -0.01049, -0.00955 and -0.00438. Permitting at most
two unwanted entries still does not recover them. Their existing provisional
rationales concern broad human communication, journalism and language; those
rationales do not establish matching reference families in this music-heavy
set. A boundary alone cannot resolve these comparisons under the supplied
controls. Missing suitable references and score limitations remain possible;
this calculation distinguishes neither and changes no judgment.

### Consequence, verification and integration

The existing meaning scores permit substantial improvement on these cases.
That supports continuing automatic-boundary investigation with the current
representation and fixed groups, rather than changing vectors again. It does
**not** locate the successful ranges from accepted references alone. The next
suggested investigation is to inspect the six problematic HR36 groups'
accepted-reference support curves against their feasible ranges, retaining
Sports/AI as over-tightening controls. Do not deploy label-derived boundaries
or tune separate constants per group. HR37 can test a resulting reference-only
rule and HR38 remains outside development. That next test is not started here.

All **273 reviewed panel rows** replay old predictions exactly. Independent
scalar comparisons verify **49,902** useful/unwanted/group pairs; **7,672**
permitted-unwanted subsets are enumerated. Query/group permutation,
useful-duplicate, threshold-realization, zero-error, monotonic-budget and
tightening-subset checks pass. The seven existing production
`ScoreSeparationTests` pass. All source/assessment hashes remain unchanged;
core execution takes **0.22 seconds**. No new production APIs or modules.

Keep `panel-results.json` with original labels and panel-only hypothetical
witnesses; `all-useful-group-routes.json` with every useful/group score and
blocker; `blocked-useful-cases.json` with the three Arts cases; and complete
reviewed score matrices, source bindings and gap details. Hypothetical
boundaries are explicitly diagnostic and never exported as runtime policy or
applied to the full omitted corpus. `verification.json`, calculation-script
hashes and `review.md` preserve reproduction. Land these maintained findings;
keep generated evidence ignored and retain the task worktree for frozen paths.
No LLMs, training, new encodings, paid resources, new labels, prompts, official
hierarchy artifacts, spreadsheet entries or UI changes.

## HR36 reference-curve diagnosis: sparse constraints and an isolation clue

The human approved inspecting accepted-reference signals in the six problematic
HR36 groups, with all outsider scores and judgments fixed. No replacement
boundary is implemented or adopted. Evidence:
`target/food-halves-v1/hr36-reference-curves-v1/` in the retained food-halves
worktree. Frozen plan SHA256
`6d7c07733f71331266fd2eb36837c1860ded113357fcadab111267788d3c162f`.
The six groups were selected from known failures: this is targeted development
evidence, not independent validation. Sports and broad AI are unchanged controls.

### The previous 28/28 result has only five dependent useful cases

Recheck each useful entry's passing groups under the earlier tightening-only
zero-error witness. **Only five of 28 useful cases depend on any adjusted
group**; the other 23 already have routes through unchanged groups:

- Books can use Photography at or below 0.017844 **or** Technology at or below
  0.014706. Each is an alternative to the other; both cannot be disabled.
- Arsenals and Ukrainian forces depend on the Defence group.
- LLMs and chatbots depend on the AI models/agents group.
- None of the five useful Sports cases depends on Gaming, the group that
  admitted the unwanted software cases. The Bitcoin initiative/media group
  likewise has no dependent useful outsider in this sample.

The earlier mathematical ceiling remains correct. But it does not validate
replacement boundaries for every group: disabling some whole groups would
appear successful because the sample contains no useful outsider needing them.
Preserve original valid member meanings as controls too, including the rare
roleplaying-game meanings. No provisional label is changed.

### Accepted-member gaps do not locate a shared boundary

Calculate every accepted root entry's unchanged mean meaning affinity to each
fixed target/control group, excluding its own source note. Preserve full
member/nonmember score curves, with one cumulative unit of mass per source note,
and all raw/log adjacent gaps. Other accepted groups are comparison evidence,
**not semantic-negative labels**. Save the reference-only results before
joining the previous label-dependent diagnostic ranges.

| Problematic group | Must exceed reviewed unwanted score | Constraint from uniquely dependent useful cases |
|---|---:|---|
| C00001 Photography | 0.010260 | Books has the Technology alternative |
| C00001 Technology | 0.013730 | Books has the Photography alternative |
| C00003 Defence | 0.021150 | At or below Arsenals' 0.037675 |
| C00004 Bitcoin initiative/media | 0.034077 | No dependent useful outsider |
| C00005 Gaming | 0.049963 | No dependent useful outsider |
| C00006 AI models/agents | 0.124425 | At or below LLMs' 0.140704 |

These ranges use known judgments and other groups' saved witness boundaries.
They are not automatic numerical settings. No individual upper bound for
Photography or Technology means neither can be varied independently without
considering Books' alternative-route constraint.

There is no consistent largest-gap signal across these distributions:

- Defence's largest member log gap is 0.017501–0.044096; geometric midpoint
  0.027780 falls inside its retrospective feasible range.
- Gaming's largest member log-gap midpoint is 0.036480, **below** the unwanted
  maximum 0.049963; it would not reject the software cases.
- AI's largest gap is 0.139812–0.219318, midpoint **0.175109**. That lies above
  both LLMs (0.140704) and chatbots (0.166945). It separates accepted-subtopic
  variation, not unwanted outsiders.
- The full-reference Photography curve's lower bound lies in the small
  0.010222–0.010300 gap, rank 95 among log gaps. Technology's is inside
  0.013726–0.013738, rank 645. These are dense reference regions, not dominant
  breaks. A group-member-only gap does not solve the full-reference problem.

The existing anchored-neighbor bend is unavailable for five of the six target
groups. Its Bitcoin-group boundary 0.010801 is below the old minimum and the
unwanted scores. All historical curves and failed member-bend controls remain
unchanged. Another uniform largest-gap/member-bend adjustment is not justified
by this evidence; this does not prove every reference-only estimator impossible.

### Exploratory clue: raw closest-peer meaning agreement

After that planned inspection, freeze a separate exploratory nearest-peer
check, SHA256
`270cb71df14fba5a1b9bee7d34e008ede27412623cd96fa5118ce8eca79d0541`.
For all 126 members of the eight target/control groups, record the maximum
meaning-vector cosine to a same-group member from **another source note**.
This uses raw similarities before mutual-neighbor pruning, and does not alter
outsider scoring, fixed memberships or the accepted graph.

| Existing reference | Closest cross-note meaning cosine | Comparison |
|---|---:|---|
| IRIB in Photography | 0.15949 | Next least-connected member: Imago at 0.54657 |
| Technology: named posting destination | 0.23124 | Next least-connected member: Technology at 0.71322 |
| AI agents, L04632 | 0.69301 | Valid specialized agents, despite zero mutual meaning edges |
| AI agents, L02645 | 0.77778 | Valid agents setting the group's lowest mean support |
| Roleplaying-game character backgrounds | 0.42964 | Valid, rare narrower topic with weaker cross-note support |

This separates the two conspicuous isolated-reference examples from the valid
low-mean AI agents; mutual graph-edge absence did not. It is a potential
reference-quality signal, **not an admission rule or a universal cosine cutoff**.
Rare valid topics also have weaker support. Even correctly identifying an
isolated reference does not determine the next boundary: deleting IRIB and
using the next Photography member's mean 0.046524 would exceed Books' 0.017844
route. Technology's next minimum 0.127563 likewise exceeds Books' alternative.
No reference is deleted and no next-minimum rule is adopted.

The next proposed investigation is whether the existing meaning-distance
clustering, applied within the fixed groups only as a diagnostic, isolates
IRIB/the Technology destination while retaining valid AI agents and rare
roleplaying topics. Track source-note identities so several entries from one
post are not presented as independent support. Keep canonical/reference
membership and outsider mean scores unchanged. This would qualify the isolation
signal before defining an admission boundary, not supply a complete solution itself. That clustering
investigation has not been run here; do not imply an approved policy change.

### Verification and saved evidence

Eight groups produce **2,282 accepted-reference scores**. All **126 existing
member scores** replay exactly. Independent scalar cosine/kernel means check
3,424 pairs, maximum error `4.44e-16`. Curve note mass totals one per source
note. The overlay reproduces 53 panel constraints, including repeated C00001
constraints. All four saved reference-stage file hashes remain unchanged after
the overlay, and all source/assessment hashes remain unchanged. Core calculation
takes **0.35 seconds**. The exploratory nearest-peer inspection checks all 126
scalar maxima within `1e-14` and exactly reproduces its retained result bytes.

Keep complete accepted-reference scores, curves and gaps; original model
summaries; all diagnostic outsider overlays; joint useful-route dependencies;
and every closest-peer value/identity. `evaluation-sources.json` pins the ignored
calculation scripts; `verification.json` and `review.md` preserve checks and the
compact report. No new production code, APIs, tests or runtime components.
No LLMs, training, encodings, paid resources, hierarchy/prompt/UI changes,
spreadsheet updates or HR37/38 execution. Land the maintained findings on local
`main`; retain the task worktree for frozen evidence and vector source paths.

## HR36 meaning-only subgroup diagnostic: reject the reference filter

The human approved testing whether meaning-only clustering inside the eight
saved groups distinguishes IRIB/the Technology destination from valid AI
agents and roleplaying meanings. Freeze plan SHA256
`8fd339d6c245904d6071304be55a7f095268a16def3b9173ad59caab4c06216a`.
Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/hr36-within-group-meaning-hdbscan-v1/` in the
retained food-halves worktree. **The clustering fails the valid-reference
controls. Do not adopt subgroup/noise membership as a reference-quality filter.**
No canonical membership, reference membership or outsider admission changes.

### Existing production numerical path, unchanged parameters

Run each fixed group independently on its saved full-meaning vectors with the
same frozen native numerical binary as HR36: exact angular HDBSCAN,
`min_samples=3` counting self, `min_cluster_size=2`, EOM. There are no outsiders,
title vectors, synthetic vectors, cross-group pooling or parameter variants.
Production `hierarchy_hdbscan.request_for` and `members` bind the controls,
full-meaning vector identities, normalized bytes, aliases and expanded output.
The ordinary tree validation also passes. Full output is used for these tiny
at-most-30-point inputs because native core/outlier values are inspected;
production hierarchy output remains compact.

| Original fixed group | Entries | Selected subgroup sizes | Distinct notes per subgroup | Ungrouped |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| C00001 Photography | 18 | 3, 10 | 3, 8 | 5 |
| C00001 Technology | 19 | None | — | 19 |
| C00003 Defence | 6 | None | — | 6 |
| C00004 Bitcoin initiative/media | 3 | None | — | 3 |
| C00005 Gaming | 20 | 15, 3 | 14, 1 | 2 |
| C00005 Sports | 15 | None | — | 15 |
| C00006 AI models/agents | 15 | 9, 3 | 9, 3 | 3 |
| C00006 broad AI | 30 | 8, 13 | 8, 13 | 9 |

Across 126 existing entries, 64 are assigned to diagnostic subgroups and 62
are ungrouped. These are geometric counts, not accuracy or changed note
coverage. Four inputs yield no selected subgroup at all.

### The desired distinction fails

Photography leaves IRIB ungrouped, but also valid Photographers, Camera lenses,
Images and Imago. AI models/agents leaves **both valid agent controls L02645
and L04632** ungrouped, plus valid AI tooling. Nine model definitions form one
subgroup; two other agents and model capabilities form another. That cannot
justify discarding the remaining agents.

The three roleplaying-game background/magic/rules entries are grouped, but
all originate from **one source note**. This is not independent cross-note
confirmation. Broad AI also leaves nine Artificial-intelligence definitions
ungrouped. Its highest GLOSH outlier score, **0.69635**, belongs to the valid
L02836 definition of the AI field and its applications. It exceeds IRIB's
**0.68845**. The native score is a geometric outlier measure, not a semantic
error probability or a universally valid exclusion cutoff.

### Root ineligibility makes all-noise results inconclusive

The existing algorithm intentionally excludes the all-data root from EOM and
leaf selection. A condensed tree with no surviving non-root cluster therefore
reports all points as noise. Technology's saved tree has zero proper child
branches: its 19 ungrouped points do not establish that all Technology meanings
are unrelated or invalid. Defence, Bitcoin and Sports similarly have no
selected subgroup. Do not change root eligibility to make this result look
successful.

Eight whole-note exclusions cover every source contributing multiple entries
inside a group. Removing the one note supplying the three roleplaying entries
leaves **17 Gaming entries and no proper branch**. All are then noise, including
15 previously grouped entries that remain present. Their topics did not become
invalid; the flat-selection structure changed. This is expected current
HDBSCAN behavior, not evidence to remove the reference collection or a generator
bug. Other exclusions switch two remaining Photography entries or individual
console entries. Withholding the two-entry AI source retains the same two
subgroups. Preserve all folds, IDs, note identities and numerical changes.

### Disposition and proposed next experiment

Reject subgroup membership as the proposed reference filter. Raw closest-peer
similarities and native outlier scores remain diagnostics; neither supplies an
automatic admission boundary here. No reference is removed, no threshold is
changed and no additional clustering settings are searched.

The next proposed bounded experiment is **local distance scaling for admission
affinities**. Fusion already computes nearby-reference scales, while admission
uses one global `exp(-5.5*(1-cosine))` scale. Compare a reference's distances
relative to its accepted meaning neighbors, keeping the full-meaning vectors,
fixed fusion groups, independent outsiders, mean group aggregation and the
existing weakest-member boundary. Change only similarity scaling, then measure
actual automatic decisions on the frozen panels rather than a hindsight cutoff
ceiling. This tests calibration without deciding which accepted entries are
semantically bad. It is a hypothesis, not evidence of improvement or approval
to deploy. Freeze the exact normalization and source-note controls before any
such calculation; no part of it is implemented or run in this investigation.

### Verification and evidence

**18 native numerical invocations** comprise eight baselines, eight duplicate-
source-note exclusions and two reversed-input controls. Technology and Gaming
permutations preserve expanded subgroups and every per-entry numerical result
exactly. The pinned HR36 binary SHA256 is
`448aa03e6f35a7cea3d643f91d33135d9c3c4710f0aeee24711081f6b02652d7`.
Independent scalar angular-core calculations differ by at most `7.33e-15`;
independent recursive GLOSH reconstruction is exact. All source and vector
hashes remain unchanged. Native process time is **0.042 seconds**, full
numerical evaluation **0.73 seconds**. Eleven existing production HDBSCAN
adapter tests pass; no new testing seam or production API is added.

`native/` retains every source-bound request/result and stderr;
`base-groups.json`, `duplicate-note-jackknives.json`, `all-native-runs.json`,
`focal-case-results.json`, `input-permutation-checks.json` and
`vector-bindings.json` retain complete output and source bindings. Keep the
script hash, `verification.json` and `review.md`. Generated artifacts stay
ignored; maintained findings land on local `main`, with the task worktree
retained for frozen evidence/vector paths. No model calls, training, encodings,
paid resources, prompts, hierarchy/UI changes, official hierarchy-run records,
spreadsheet updates or HR37/38 execution.

## Local angular admission scaling: reject the query-adaptive variant

The human approved the preceding local-distance experiment. **This one frozen
variant fails actual automatic admissions. Do not adopt it.** In particular,
the Arts reference collection admits every tested outsider. Larger proposal
counts therefore do not demonstrate improved coverage or quality. The current
hierarchy, reference collections, fusion groups and admission policy are
unchanged.

Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/local-meaning-affinity-v1/` in the retained
food-halves worktree. Plan SHA256
`7f2627b3094460b8d456185e80d5a10e52989ed744f5c32c9aacdddf8570c937`;
implementation manifest SHA256
`f6db4a4ddfce65b5a7daaf98cfa1abf43f26a06fa8d9045a3c8314cfd3073816`.
Both were frozen before evaluating the dataset. No parameter search, reference
deletion, regrouping, label change or second panel variant was performed.

### Exact frozen calculation

Use saved normalized full-meaning vectors and angular distance
`d = acos(clipped cosine)`. For each entry, define `rho` as its mean distance to
the nearest 20 distinct other source notes among the fixed accepted references.
Each note contributes its nearest entry only; exclude the entry's whole source
note. Use all other notes if fewer than 20 exist. No other note means
unavailable; an exactly zero radius uses the existing machine-epsilon floor.

Reference radii are computed once, including the same accepted context-only
references as before. Each outsider independently obtains its own radius from
these fixed references, never from other outsiders. It does not enter a graph
or change any reference radius. For an entry pair, calculate:

```text
s = 0.5 * (rho_i + rho_j + d) / 3
affinity = 2*sqrt(rho_i*rho_j)/(rho_i+rho_j+d) * exp(-0.5*(d/s)^2)
```

This adapts the existing fusion local Gaussian form, with distinct-note
neighbors and self-weight normalization into [0,1]. It is not the unchanged
fusion algorithm or its sparse mutual-neighbor graph. Keep original fusion
groups, cross-note mean aggregation and the original minimum-member boundary
rule. Recalculate that boundary using these affinities; use no extra gate,
cutoff, bend or label-derived threshold. Calibration still uses the full fixed
reference collection, so it is not an independent held-out accuracy estimate.

### Matched automatic results

Run all 1,449 saved HR35 query entries against its Food/Arts references, and
all 1,121 HR36 omitted entries against those references and the six native
HR36 roots: **197 fixed groups and 278,797 query/group scores**. Keep development
panels separate; their provisional entry judgments are not new human gold.
The table counts selected entries, with useful and unwanted denominators fixed.

| Panel | Useful selected, old → local | Unwanted selected, old → local |
|---|---:|---:|
| HR35 Food, 30 useful / 51 unwanted | 14 → 14 | 3 → 4 |
| HR35 expanded Arts, 63 / 30 | 54 → 63 | 10 → 30 |
| HR36 queries against old Food, 19 / 62 | 15 → 16 | 1 → 5 |
| HR36 queries against old Arts, 18 / 57 | 5 → 18 | 1 → 57 |

Arts selects all 1,449 HR35 queries and all 1,121 HR36 queries. Food proposals
increase from 43 to 58 HR35 entries and 39 to 59 HR36 entries, without improved
reviewed precision. Newly selected entries outside the old 39-entry HR36 Food
audit are retained **unreviewed**; do not claim the expanded proposals were fully
audited. On the old audit subset, 25/30 useful, 7/7 unwanted and 2/2 unresolved
headings remain selected. All eleven useful and five unwanted notes still have
some selected route.

The 64-case native HR36 post-selection audit changes useful selection from
36/36 to 33/36 while retaining all 14 unwanted cases; unresolved selection
changes 14/14 to 13/14. The lost useful entries are Social and political power
L01290, Videos L03929 and Large language models L01172. These are existing
provisional judgments, not a newly reviewed sample or whole-note certification.

All four explicit **Food whole-note** decisions still match: the three accepted
Eggs/Egg yolks/Easter eggs notes retain a route; the rejected Easter broadcast
note remains excluded. This does not approve every heading or rescue the wider
result. L00440 Egg yolks loses its individual route, while L00438 from that note
still passes. Higher retrieval counts for Arts reflect admitting every query,
not successful discrimination.

### Demonstrated failure mechanism, with a bounded counterfactual

The Albums/Music group `HR35:C00003/fused-008` has nine accepted entries. Its
weakest member, general Music L02400, sets a local boundary of 0.0156223. This
group alone admits all 1,121 HR36 queries and 1,448/1,449 HR35 queries. A further
group admits the remaining HR35 entry. Native HR36 Asset reserves/Assets
`fused-014` admits 1,004/1,121; Defence `fused-002` admits 1,059/1,121.

An outsider far from the references receives a larger radius precisely
because those reference distances are large. That can make the comparison more
permissive than the reference-member comparisons that establish its boundary.
Fixed reference graphs and independent outsiders do not prevent this effect.

Freeze a post-result two-case mechanism diagnostic before calculation
(`failure-trace-plan.json`, SHA256
`ad34b92a64a6fa93af92ab7d950ec7b79002eae8abde1809fd52a5fd73c82119`).
Keep query vectors, reference vectors/radii, group means and boundaries fixed;
replace only the query radius by the radius of the weakest reference setting
that boundary. This is a counterfactual diagnostic, **not a second policy or
panel trial**; it chooses no cutoff or new semantic label.

| Existing unwanted case | Actual query radius | Weak reference radius | Actual score | Counterfactual score | Fixed boundary |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Software inputs L02670 → Albums/Music | 1.19989 | 0.49362 | 0.035278 | 0.005372 | 0.015622 |
| Logistics L02900 → Defence | 1.17455 | 0.96063 | 0.047660 | 0.033009 | 0.029957 |

Software inputs changes from admitted to rejected solely through that radius
change. Logistics remains admitted. Query-side expansion is therefore a
demonstrated mechanism, not the only explanation or proof that reference-only
scaling would solve admission generally. Absolute boundary values from
different kernels are not directly comparable evidence of quality.

### Verification, disposition and next hypothesis

The new experimental module is `sse_multivector/fixed_reference_local_scaling.py`.
Nine failing-first numerical tests exercise its actual functions and existing
group-mean/boundary paths; all **54 relevant numerical regression tests pass**.
Baseline scores, boundaries and exact group/root decisions replay. Independent
checks cover 4,607 scalar group means, 3,042 scalar affinities and 234 neighbor
radii, with maximum absolute errors below `4.5e-16`. Query isolation, reordering
and duplication preserve results; reference bytes and all protected inputs
remain unchanged. Numerical execution took 5.19 seconds. Passing arithmetic
checks does not establish semantic quality.

Reject this exact query-adaptive variant without tuning it to the observed
failures. The next proposed experiment is to derive comparison scales only
from accepted reference members, so an outsider cannot relax its own allowance
by being farther away. That is unimplemented and untested, and the Logistics
counterfactual cautions against expecting a complete solution. Freeze any such
next calculation before evaluating it; wait for separate user approval.

Keep `reference-models.json.gz`, complete score matrices, `neighbor-scales.json`,
cohort/audit/human-note reports, `failure-group-summary.json`, per-reference
`failure-trace.json`, `verification.json` and the compact `review.md`.
`evaluation-sources.json` pins numerical scripts and frozen manifests. Generated
evidence stays ignored; reusable experimental code/tests and maintained
findings land on local `main`, without activation. Retain the task worktree
for frozen source vectors and evidence paths. No LLM calls, training, new
encodings, paid resources, hierarchy/prompt/UI changes, official-run spreadsheet
updates or HR37/38 execution.

## Fixed group scales: no improvement over the original admission rule

The human approved deriving scales only from accepted entries after the failed
query-adaptive experiment. **One fixed common scale per fusion group removes
the admit-everything failure, but does not improve the original baseline. Do
not adopt it.** This result is not an unseen-corpus validation, a hierarchy
change or a claim that all reference-only methods fail.

Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/fixed-group-meaning-affinity-v1/` in the retained
food-halves worktree. Freeze plan SHA256
`6656c5aa0622c77ca48f051c03d20d09d64aca4826dfe1afa48e5632a74b7a68` and
implementation manifest SHA256
`ab08af2391a80b0624a5e7b583602c453c679f3de4c2ec0b635baf431a68df48`
before dataset outcomes. Reuse the existing tested numerical functions; no new
production component or source change is necessary.

### Exactly what changes

Keep the preceding root-reference radii: mean angular distance to the nearest
20 distinct other source notes among accepted references, with one nearest
entry per note and the entire scored note excluded. Keep `sigma=0.5`, the
normalized Gaussian formula, full-meaning vectors, fixed fusion groups,
cross-note group means and the minimum-member boundary rule.

For each group, take the ordinary median of its original members' reference
radii. Supply this same radius for both endpoints of every member/member and
outsider/member comparison in that group. Thus calibration and queries use
the identical symmetric metric. No outsider distance, label, prior answer or
synthetic root vector selects its scale. This replaces heterogeneous endpoint
radii with a common group radius; it is not a test of a directed kernel with
different scales per reference. Group medians and means retain their ordinary
entry weighting; do not claim a new duplicate-invariance property for them.

No reference is removed, group is rebuilt, boundary rule is added or parameter
is searched. Recalculate boundaries in the new metric rather than comparing
new scores against old-kernel threshold values.

### Actual automatic outcomes

Use all the same 1,449 HR35 queries and 1,121 HR36 omitted entries: **197 groups
and 278,797 query/group scores**. Compare with the original global-kernel
baseline, not only the disastrous query-adaptive variant. The table counts
selected entries under existing provisional judgments.

| HR35 expanded Arts, 63 / 30 | 54 → 49 | 10 → 9 |
| HR36 queries against old Food, 19 / 62 | 15 → 15 | 1 → 1 |
| HR36 queries against old Arts, 18 / 57 | 5 → 4 | 1 → 1 |

No useful example is newly selected in these panels. HR35 Arts loses National
flags L01660, Photography L04834, Photos L05857, Video gaming L06169 and Academy
Awards L06413; HR36 Arts loses Films L04941. The one removed HR35 unwanted
case is Wondering L00538. These are existing agent assessments, not new human
decisions or semantic certification.

HR36 Food selects exactly the same 39 headings and 16 notes as the original
baseline; its old complete proposal audit remains 30 useful / 7 unwanted /
2 unresolved headings and 11 useful / 5 unwanted notes. The six-native-root
64-case audit changes useful selection **36 → 35**, retaining **all 14 unwanted
cases**; unresolved selection is 14 → 13. The useful loss is Social and
political power L01290. All four explicit Food whole-note human decisions
still match, with the original selected heading routes restored. Preserve

Raw proposal counts alone are not quality evidence. HR35 Food changes 43 → 41

C00001 through C00006 respectively change 702 → 695, 11 → 10, 39 → 35,

### Five concrete limits of common similarity rescaling

Freeze a separate **post-result diagnostic**, not a second admission variant:
`dominance-diagnostic-plan.json`, SHA256
`72ed2f60da9d46dff127bc73b1d66cd19bbb850bd3b519f33bc1309b433fb1e2`.
Inspect all 28 selected unwanted root/query controls across these panels and
the native-root audit, retaining every passing group/member comparison.

For each query, compare its cosine distribution over group members with each
member's cosine distribution over its other-note peers. Use the same uniform
entry weights as actual group means. In five cases the outsider's distribution
is stronger at every quantile than that of the accepted member setting the
minimum boundary:

| Existing unwanted outsider | Reference collection | Accepted boundary-setting member |
|---|---|---|
| HR35 L02274 Visits to Japan | Food, fused-004 | L05006 Dining groups |
| HR35 L03286 Invitations | Food, fused-004 | L05006 Dining groups |
| HR35 L03969 Vector arithmetic | Arts, fused-021 | L05675 Trying soundpacks |
| HR35 L04878 Spaces in configuration text | Arts, fused-021 | L05675 Trying soundpacks |
| HR36 L05143 Diets | Native C00001, fused-071 | L01694 Technology posting destination |

For these five cases, **any common per-group increasing cosine-to-affinity
transform gives the outsider at least that member's mean**. The minimum-member
boundary is no higher than the member's mean, so the outsider must pass.
Changing this kind of distance scale cannot reject them while keeping the same
vectors, groups, mean aggregation and minimum-member rule. This is conditional
on the existing unwanted judgments and reference memberships; it does not
establish that a reference is semantically invalid or should be deleted.

Verify this finite-distribution relation two ways: exact empirical CDF counts
using integer cross-products, and independent quantiles over rational
probability intervals. All 425 member comparisons agree. Five comparisons
satisfy the condition, one for each of five queries; all five members also
actually set the current boundary. Their smallest cosine quantile advantages
range 0.00303–0.02323, beyond numerical roundoff. Complete distributions,
identities and actual baseline/fixed-scale scores are retained. Absence of
this sufficient condition in the other 23 cases does not prove that changing a
kernel would fix them. Different endpoint-dependent transforms and different
aggregation or boundary rules are outside this argument.

### Verification and next scope

All **54 existing relevant numerical tests pass**; no tested production source
is changed. Baseline scores, minima and exact group/root predictions replay.
Independent checks cover all 1,689 reference radii, 197 group medians, 24,325
scalar affinities and 4,607 scalar group means. Maximum absolute numerical
errors are below `4.5e-16`. Queries evaluated separately, reordered or
duplicated preserve their results; reference/group scales and protected source
hashes stay fixed. Numerical evaluation took 4.16 seconds.

Do not adopt or tune this common-scale variant. The next useful investigation
is the minimum-member admission assumption exposed by those five controls,
rather than another common scale. An existing accepted member remaining in
the hierarchy does not itself justify letting that one member set the standard
for every new outsider. No replacement boundary or other variant is implemented
or authorized by these findings.

Keep the frozen plan/implementation, score matrices, reference models/scales,
cohort/audit/human reports, `dominance-diagnostic-results.json`,
`dominance-witness-score-table.json`, `verification.json`, script hashes and
`review.md` as ignored evidence. Land maintained findings on local `main` and
retain the worktree for frozen vector/evidence paths. No LLM calls, training,
new encodings, paid resources, prompt/hierarchy/UI changes, official hierarchy
run or spreadsheet updates, or HR37/38 execution.

## Reference-note coverage boundary: limited measured improvement

The human approved revisiting the weakest-member admission assumption. Test
one replacement that preserves a route for each eligible **reference note**,
rather than making every accepted heading set an admission standard. **This
improves several saved panels, with two useful Arts heading losses. It is an
experimental candidate, not an adopted policy or a solved admission rule.**

Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/note-coverage-boundary-v1/` in the retained
food-halves worktree. Plan SHA256
`ff5b55e7fd67b5fd38195075c4d057b09b604f262247ff9b2ff3da00e0482e03` and
implementation manifest SHA256
`2e5ceaf4706cc1a5dad2c7adae43cc45fc90019e512ad91808c88c1b45bbee9e`
were frozen before changed dataset outcomes. Keep original global-kernel
scores, vectors, fixed fusion memberships and all previous judgments. Do not
reuse the failed common-scale scores or repeat the failed member-score bend.
No outsider, semantic label, parameter scan or fitted model chooses a boundary.

### A note needs one route; every heading need not define the boundary

For each eligible group, retain each member's original mean affinity to that
group, already excluding the member's entire source note. Within each source
note, take its **best member score** in that group. The median of those
per-note best scores describes the group's usual reference support, giving
each represented source note one value.

For each note, divide its best score in each of its original groups by that
group's median; retain the note's highest ratio across groups. The smallest
of those note-best ratios is the largest common relative requirement that
still gives every such note a route. Raise each group's old boundary to this
requirement times its own median, whenever that is higher:

```text
a[g,n] = max original member score for note n in group g
m[g]   = median of a[g,n] over notes represented in g
r[n]   = max a[g,n] / m[g] over n's original eligible groups
t      = min r[n] over those reference notes
new_boundary[g] = max(old_boundary[g], t * m[g])
```

There is no chosen numerical acceptance constant. Medians scale support;
they are not an acceptance percentile or semantic exclusion label. Calibrate
using original group memberships only, rather than letting a reference find
an unestablished route through an unrelated group. Missing cross-note groups
stay unavailable; nonpositive medians retain their old boundary and do not
supply ratios. No such zero-median case occurs in this dataset. Thresholds
above one are allowed to make outsider expansion unavailable through a group;
they do not delete that group or any accepted member. None exceeds one here.

The rule can only tighten prior boundaries. Every originally covered reference
note still has at least one member score above a new boundary, because its
best ratio is at least t and every member score already met the old minimum.
This proves **reference self-consistency**, not recovery of unseen valid
topics. Original members and canonical evidence remain unchanged even when
their own member score falls below the new outsider boundary.

Replay the same **197 groups and 278,797 saved query/group scores**, covering
1,449 HR35 queries and 1,121 HR36 omitted entries. The table reports selected
entries on overlapping, provisional development panels; do not pool accuracy
or promote these labels to human gold.

| Existing reviewed panel | Useful selected, old → note coverage | Unwanted selected, old → note coverage |

| HR35 expanded Arts, 63 / 30 | 54 → 52 | 10 → 4 |
| HR36 queries against old Food, 19 / 62 | 15 → 15 | 1 → 1 |
| HR36 queries against old Arts, 18 / 57 | 5 → 5 | 1 → 0 |
| Native HR36 post-selection audit, 36 / 14 | 36 → 36 | 14 → 12 |

complete old Food-proposal audit remains complete under this subset rule:

notes **11 → 11**, unwanted notes **5 → 3**. Rewards L01349 and Return visits
L04835 are the removed unwanted Food headings. These are existing provisional
judgments. The separate pre-score useful-note retrieval counts stay 8/10 for

The two useful Arts heading losses are Gaming L01596 and Video gaming L06169.

### Why the AI change works, and what remains unfixed

Only **seven of 197 group boundaries change**. For native HR36 AI models/agents,
the old minimum is 0.1081345 from AI agents L02645. The same source note has
AI models L02650 scoring 0.3948783, so that weak heading need not set the bar
to preserve the note. Another independent agent entry, L04632, now limits
the group's requirement at **0.1350310**. Every eligible reference note retains
a route.
The two useful AI outsider controls survive, while Intelligence operations
L00905 and Random selection L04521 are rejected. This boundary was derived
from accepted references, not selected using those outsider scores or labels.

In Food, Dining groups L05006 stops setting the lowest standard while the
same note retains Restaurants, Restaurant staff, menus and dining-experience
routes. Trying soundpacks L05675 likewise has the stronger Soundpacks L05674
route from its note. However, **all five earlier cosine-distribution
counterexamples still pass**. Raising their boundaries enough would require
more than this particular reference-coverage rule permits. Do not claim the
motivating five failures were solved by the wider panel improvement.

The remaining limit is visible in native C00001: IRIB's note has just its
Photography-group route and limits the root multiplier to **0.0355987**.
The Technology threshold rises only 0.0101199 → 0.0103780; its unwanted Diets
match remains. Preserving each reference note's self-recovery can still let
one difficult note limit the other groups. This does not justify deleting
IRIB, changing its canonical membership or inventing a semantic exclusion rule.

### Verification and next test

Eight failing-first tests exercise the new reusable experimental numerical
function `sse_multivector/fixed_reference_note_coverage.py` through actual mean
scoring and member-boundary inputs. All **70 relevant numerical tests pass**.
Exact rational ratio comparisons/multiplication preserve limiting float ties
without an epsilon. Tests also cover independent note mass, duplicated
identical members, permutations, unavailable/zero groups, above-one thresholds,
unchanged inputs and invalid scores. There is no constructor/runner seam or
production activation.

Saved query scores remain unchanged; every old minimum and group/root
prediction replays. Independent integer cross-products and 120-digit decimal
multiplication reproduce all new
boundaries with **zero scalar discrepancy**. All **982 eligible source-note/root
pairs** retain a member route. Another 59 reference-note/root pairs have no
eligible original group route and are outside that guarantee; they are not
removed or silently counted as recovered. Identical-member duplication and
group/member permutations preserve the new calculation on frozen member-score
inputs; this does not establish duplication invariance of the underlying mean
scorer when actual references change. Root calibrations are saved before
query scores are evaluated and their hashes stay unchanged afterward.
All proposals are subsets of old proposals; no new unwanted admission is
possible under this exact score-preserving tightening. Numerical replay takes
**0.60 seconds**, using only saved scores. All protected sources remain unchanged.

Freeze this candidate and transfer it **unchanged to HR37 next**, before
tuning the gaming losses or remaining errors. That is the next recommended
test, not performed here. The current evidence is development evidence after
many prior experiments; it does not establish generalization. No automatic
adoption or change to existing hierarchy membership is authorized by the result.

Keep reference models, `root-calibrations.json`, all per-note retained/lost

## HR37 transfer: note coverage tightens admission, with useful losses

The human authorized testing the preceding reference-note boundary **unchanged
on HR37**, including local frozen SSE encoding. This transfer removes unwanted
matches, but also loses useful matches. **It does not establish preserved
hierarchy quality or justify automatic adoption.** No rule, score, parameter,
reference membership, prompt or hierarchy was changed to improve these results.

Evidence: `target/food-halves-v1/hr37-note-coverage-transfer-v1/` in the retained
food-halves worktree. Frozen plan SHA256
`0b398f34f761f34898947b03873a99007dddeb73cc6dd9ebacc7eed84e21cfac`;
pre-score judgments SHA256
`89c1fd4652d066ab4e58d71ed67e633c80cd29db12ef46ecfe9879af73b66843`;
native baseline-selected audit judgments SHA256
`59dab5a83b13a7b1f94d89c8e9f7df490946ed700beaceff7b9083f63ad4bca5`.
The candidate remains the exact function from `014cb381`.

### Fresh notes, fixed references, independently evaluated outsiders

HR37 has 1,000 notes: 622 already represented, 378 omitted. Of the omitted
notes, 290 supply 1,279 usable title/meaning entries; the other 88 have no usable
meaning and cannot be placed by this test. HR37 shares no source-note IDs with
either HR35's or HR36's complete 1,000-note corpus. Repeated definition text
can still occur; fresh note identity does not imply novel semantic wording.

Use only each HR37 root's original first-version entries, without later
amendments, descendants or synthetic representatives. The six collections have
388, 297, 93, 54, 194 and 77 entries, respectively. Retain the two old Food/Arts
reference collections and their exact groups/boundaries as separate transfer
controls. These are eight reference collections, not eight roots of one graph.

Encode the entire explicit meaning and, separately, the title in its original
`title + two newlines + meaning` context. Reuse 47 exact saved entry encodings;
encode 2,325 new contexts plus nine exact-replay controls, yielding 4,668 local
SSE rows. Use the same frozen checkpoint/base artifact and actual pinned
Python/Torch/Transformers runtime, on the host CPU with 12 threads. The config's
container descriptor is provenance; this execution did not use Docker.

For each own root, retain the unchanged accepted-only title/meaning fusion:
20 neighbors, sigma 0.5, 20 exchanges, HDBSCAN minimum samples 3 and group size
2. This produces 54, 35, 3, 5, 22 and 11 fixed fusion groups. C1/C2/C3 report
disconnected-distance/infinite-MST warnings; preserve those warnings and groups,
without silently changing the algorithm. Old Food/Arts retain their 15/25 groups.
Score each outsider independently using the original mean full-meaning kernel,
`exp(-5.5 * (1 - clipped cosine))`, against each group's members. No outsiders
enter fusion or reference calibration. No synthetic-root vector is scored.

The original weakest-member boundary and the candidate see identical scores.
Freeze reference calibrations before query scoring. Preserve all accepted
entries; the changed boundaries govern outsider proposals only.

### Why boundaries change, and what note counts conceal

Only **12 of 170 boundaries change**, five of them the same old Food/Arts
boundaries already derived before HR37. Own C4/C5/C6 have no boundary change,
so their existing mistakes cannot improve under this candidate.

For own C1's Blockstr/Blockwaves group, the old weakest heading is Blockwaves:
its cross-note member mean is 0.0092268, despite a group median of 0.837851.
Its note has a stronger Bitcoin route, so it need not set the threshold to keep
that reference note. The boundary rises to **0.143374**, removing many unrelated
outsiders. The fiatjaf/Fiat currency group rises **0.0118596 → 0.0187823**;
its mixed developer/currency membership remains untouched. The United States
group rises **0.0250898 → 0.0644381**. Do not claim these numerical changes
repair the underlying group composition.

C3's large Art/Photography/Images group rises **0.0211967 → 0.0406869**. Its
weakest Images heading's note retains a stronger route; an independent
Photographers reference limits the new requirement. This removes four unwanted
audit matches and four useful ones. Reference self-recovery does not guarantee
an unseen audiovisual/design topic clears that same boundary.

Across all omitted entries, own-root proposals cover **266 → 261 distinct
notes**; this is potential expansion, not validated or applied coverage.
The five notes losing every own-root proposal concern Colorado cycling,
British Columbia daylight saving time, a named build, Cocoon Ibiza, and a
Japanese US/CPI/inflation news item. Do not label all five losses errors:

The Japanese financial note is a concrete post-result useful-loss observation,

The Japanese United States heading was already missed by the baseline; it was

### Verification, status and retained evidence

All 2,382 reconstructed titles match native HR37 title vectors within maximum

error. Receipt/model/checkpoint/request hashes, masks and every protected
source validate. Old reference thresholds replay within **6.67e-16**, while
their saved exact values remain authoritative. Independent kernels and 2,210
scalar group-mean checks match; isolation, permutations and duplicate query
checks pass. Query source notes are absent from all references.

Replay all **217,430 query/group scores** with exact comparisons. Every new
proposal is a subset of old proposals. Independent rational ratios and
120-digit decimal products reproduce all boundaries, and all **950 eligible
reference-note/root pairs** retain a route. Another 52 reference-note/root
pairs have no eligible group route and are outside that guarantee. All 76
relevant numerical tests pass. Reference construction plus baseline scoring
takes 4.67 seconds after encoding; candidate replay takes 0.10 seconds. These
exclude encoding, human/agent review and artifact preparation time.

The transfer shows real rejection improvement **with a retrieval cost**.
It strengthens evidence that note-based calibration can suppress weak-heading
admissions, while disproving any claim that its reference guarantee preserves
all useful outsider routes. Keep the candidate experimental; do not tune it
on these outcomes or call hierarchy quality preserved. Compare the saved
useful losses and unchanged-root mistakes before choosing another experiment.

native audit selections/assessments, fixed group compositions, raw score
