## HR37 mechanism diagnosis: thresholds cannot repair some score orderings

After delegating the ongoing storage relocation, resume the admission
investigation using saved HR37 vectors, fixed groups and frozen judgments.
This is a post-result diagnostic, not another automatic rule or new inference.
Evidence is in the ignored
`target/food-halves-v1/hr37-reference-pooling-diagnosis-v1/`; preliminary
ordering and individual-reference tables remain alongside the preceding
HR37 transfer evidence. The new diagnostic plan SHA256 is
`9ee745a43955bffb6c1a34433e3f938cb5821307e3e83fd028882aaa219fe09e`.

The same unwanted entry scores at least as high as a useful entry in **every
reference group** for seven of the 36 provisional useful native-audit cases.
No choice of individual group thresholds can then accept that useful entry
while rejecting that unwanted entry: whichever group accepts the useful one
also accepts the unwanted one. This is a limit of these group-mean scores and
threshold decisions, not proof that raw SSE vectors contain no useful information.

Two concrete examples, showing the original mean kernel score for the relevant
group, are:

| Own HR37 root / group | Useful entry | Score | Unwanted entry | Score |
|---|---|---:|---|---:|
| C3 / Art, photography and images | YouTube L03974 | 0.022300 | Digestion L04299 | 0.023399 |
| C4 / Games, competitions and events | Minecraft L02053 | 0.025719 | Asynchronous operations L03902 | 0.030179 |

The full saved arrays verify the same unwanted-above-useful ordering in **all**
groups of each root, not only the displayed group. Other witnesses include
Understanding above Music release dates, Film contests and Wallpaper* magazine;
Voting above Football team news; and Asynchronous operations above Film festivals.
Frozen judgments remain provisional. These are heading/group contradictions,
not seven newly lost notes or a requirement to recover every possible heading.
The larger goal remains useful note coverage with acceptable unwanted admissions.

Check the original individual connections before inventing another threshold.
For Minecraft, its closest cross-note meaning reference in the game group is
Games at cosine 0.4962, slightly above asynchronous operations' closest Events
reference at 0.4868, yet averaging the entire group reverses their order.
For YouTube, its closest meaning reference is Image sharing at 0.4713; Digestion
instead matches Creativity at 0.5216. Thus the examples expose both an averaging
effect and an already suspect individual meaning connection. Replacing every
mean with a nearest match is not established as a general repair.

Run one bounded two-by-two pooling diagnostic on **all 72 frozen native audit
cases**, not just the selected witnesses. Preserve each accepted fusion group,
its original entries, the source-note exclusion and the mean kernel formula.
Vary only which existing vectors are compared. A contextual title vector pools
title tokens with the full meaning still in encoder context; it is not a title
encoded alone. No outsider enters reference fusion, and no reference is removed.

| Incoming pooling | Reference pooling | Useful entries outranked by one unwanted entry in every group |
|---|---|---:|
| Full meaning | Full meaning | 7 / 36 |
| Full meaning | Contextual title | 3 / 36 |
| Contextual title | Full meaning | 3 / 36 |
| Contextual title | Contextual title | 5 / 36 |

These are **ordering obstructions, not admission results or accuracy**. The
meaning-to-title comparison removes all such obstructions in the News and
Activities collections and for Wallpaper* magazine. YouTube, Music release
dates and Film contests remain blocked in C3. Individual pair tradeoffs remain:
it removes seven of the ten original unwanted-dominance witnesses but introduces
six different witnesses among those remaining three useful cases. All identity
changes are saved; do not report only the improved distinct-entry count.

This supports a bounded next hypothesis: keep the incoming full meaning and
test contextual title pooling **only on the accepted-reference side**. It does
not support immediately switching admission. Existing title-only regressions
and Food role controls remain necessary. The comparison is directional:
meaning(i)-to-title(j) need not equal meaning(j)-to-title(i). Any subsequent
automatic test must compute member support in that same direction, rather
than reuse old meaning-to-meaning thresholds or symmetrize a different score.
The present production member-boundary helper requires a symmetric matrix;
that contract has not been changed here.

The 6,240 group scores have independent scalar verification within 5.56e-17;
the original meaning-to-meaning scores replay exactly. All protected source
hashes and frozen judgments stay unchanged. Runtime is 3.52 seconds after
loading existing vectors. No thresholds are fitted, no admission decisions
made, no new vectors encoded, and no LLM calls, training, hierarchy/UI changes
or HR38 use occur. Retain full arrays, individual pair changes, source hashes,
`results.json` and `verification.json`; the task worktree remains necessary
for those evidence paths, now backed by external storage through its symlink.

## Directional admission replay: better ordering did not transfer to better admission

Status: **experimental negative result; do not adopt meaning-to-title admission**.
This follows the user's approval to test the crossed representation with its own
consistently calculated boundaries. No LLM calls, new encodings, training,
clustering, hierarchy edits or production-policy changes occurred.

The previous two-by-two study measured ordering obstructions, not actual
admissions. This replay tests the actual rules on saved HR35, HR36 and HR37
vectors and fixed accepted-only fusion groups. Incoming entries pool their full
explicit meaning. Reference entries pool their title while the same explicit
meaning remains encoder context; these are not subject-only vectors. Neither
side uses a synthetic root vector or an outsider network.

For this crossed comparison, each member's calibration score uses its meaning
against other-source-note reference titles, in exactly the direction later used
for outsiders. The kernel remains `exp(-5.5 * (1 - cosine))`. The reference matrix
is intentionally asymmetric: do not transpose, symmetrize, or reuse the old
meaning-to-meaning thresholds. Both existing boundary rules are tested: weakest
cross-note member, and the reference-note-coverage rule. The latter preserves
one eligible accepted-reference route per note; that self-consistency does not
promise correct outsider admission.

The numerical helper `fixed_reference_members.derive_boundary` now accepts
aligned square directed affinities and preserves row-to-column orientation.
This removes its unnecessary symmetry restriction without changing symmetric
inputs. A production-function test first failed on the old symmetry rejection;
the new test also distinguishes the transposed result and excludes every entry
from the scored member's own note. The other boundary/graph helpers are unchanged.
Historical experiment plans, receipts and source hashes remain unchanged;
this new plan pins the revised helper and records its diff from `2fa5aebb`.

### Concrete boundary failures and gains

The old Food `fused-006` consists of ten entries titled **Food**, each retaining
its own meaning as context. Its crossed threshold is `0.007731677810394918`.
The weakest member is Food `L00696`. The following incoming meanings all pass:

| Incoming entry | Crossed group score | Existing judgment |
|---|---:|---|
| Coffee · L00228 | 0.008450 | Useful |
| Support · L02231 | 0.011017 | Unwanted |
| Online content feeds · L02234 | 0.008593 | Unwanted |
| Blood · L06803 | 0.007836 | Unwanted |

Support and Online content feeds are from the **user-rejected Easter note**
whose recorded example is `L02222`. The Easter heading itself is not admitted;
the note is incorrectly reached through those other headings. All three prior
human-accepted Food notes still pass, but this fourth explicit decision now
fails. Labels have not been reinterpreted to excuse the result. Within this
particular group's scalar scores, raising its threshold cannot keep Coffee
while rejecting the higher-scoring Support and Online content feeds.

The Arts contrast is also concrete. Its accepted Artist pages / Artists group
has crossed threshold `0.053934`; Art `L03500` scores `0.019361`, Photography
`L03656` scores `0.009747`, and Films `L05039` scores `0.008693`. Their old
meaning-to-meaning routes through this group disappear and no other group
admits them. This records the mechanism, without asserting that every lost
provisional judgment is independent human gold.

Crossing does recover useful Food topics including Coffee, Tea, Coffee beans
and Coffee roasting. It also restores the separately flagged Japanese CPI
route in HR37. Those gains are real on the fixed evidence, but do not outweigh
the explicit rejection regression and broad Arts losses as a general replacement.
The previous reduction in ordering obstructions was insufficient evidence for
changing automatic admission.

All-own-root potential omitted-note proposals rise from 239 to 244 in HR36,
and from 261 to 278 in HR37, relative to meaning-to-meaning note coverage.
These additional unreviewed proposals are **not verified note recoveries**.
No new audit selected only favorable crossed proposals.

The replay covers 14 root-reference collections, 327 fixed fusion groups,

Conclusion: keep this as a failed general replacement, not a production

## Distinct-note aggregation: useful recoveries, but a mixed whole-note tradeoff

Status: **experimental comparison; neither new score is activated**. The user
approved investigating whether whole-group averaging hides useful matches and
explicitly reminded us that perfection is unattainable. Assess practical useful
note gains and meaningful errors, rather than requiring every heading to pass
or every provisional label to be perfect.

All original full-meaning vectors, the kernel, fixed accepted-only fusion groups,
and the two existing boundary algorithms remain unchanged. The bounded replay
compares three scores; it does not search neighbor counts or choose thresholds
from semantic labels:

- **Entry mean**, the existing control: mean affinity to all eligible entries in
  the fixed group, excluding the query's whole source note.
- **Distinct-note mean**: take the strongest affinity within each accepted source
  note, then average once per note. Repeated headings from one note cannot count
  as several independent sources.
- **Closest-two-note mean**: average the strongest matches from two distinct
  accepted notes. Within each group use `k = min(2, distinct notes - 1)`, floored
  at one, for both member calibration and incoming queries. Two-source-note
  groups can calibrate against only one other note and explicitly use `k=1`;
  this is not two independent witnesses everywhere. Single-source-note groups
  would remain unavailable, rather than invent a boundary.

A new experimental numerical helper, `fixed_reference_note_support.note_support`,
implements those last two scores on the actual research calculation path.
Insufficient distinct-note support is missing, never zero or an implicit fallback.
Its tests first failed because the production function did not exist. They now
cover whole-note exclusion, insufficient support, duplicate-note invariance,
nonmember irrelevance, isolated/reordered/duplicated queries, and invalid inputs.
No encoder, fusion, hierarchy-construction or production-admission behavior changes.

Counts below deduplicate notes across all of each corpus's own roots. They are
potential proposals, not confirmed hierarchy recoveries:

| HR37 distinct-note mean | 261 | 259 | 1 | 3 |
| HR37 closest-two-note mean | 261 | 266 | 7 | 2 |

The closest-two method retains 259 of 261 existing HR37 note proposals. That
whole-note result is substantially less negative than counting every lost root
or heading as a lost note. To assess its practical tradeoff, inspect **all eleven
changed notes** across HR36 and HR37, rather than sampling favorable gains.
The saved full posts, selected meanings, actual group contents and nearest
accepted-note meanings precede separate post-result provisional assessments.
No old judgment changes, no new labels enter thresholds, and no rerun follows
these assessments. They are agent assessments, not additional human gold.

| Corpus | Gained note / admitted heading | Post-result assessment |
|---|---|---|
| HR36 | NHK news report / NHKニュース | Useful: matches accepted news-provider families. |
| HR36 | Image post / Showing legs | Unresolved: the meaning denotes physical display, without an explicit photography role. |
| HR37 | Permanent daylight saving time | Mismatch: civil clock policy does not establish the data/publication or technology/mining roles used to admit it. |
| HR37 | Dutch countryside / Netherlands | Mismatch: the country does not acquire a language-learning role by matching English/Vocabulary. |
| HR37 | `p-worm shots build #0` / Builds | Unresolved: the opaque text and generic meaning do not establish software or a game. |
| HR37 | Rugby confrontation / Physical altercations | Useful: accepted Hostility explicitly includes physical aggression. |

| HR37 | Lost Baseball note | Useful game/competition route lost. |

Thus HR36 gains one plausible useful note and one unresolved note, losing none;
HR37 gains three plausible useful notes, three mismatches and one unresolved
note, while losing two plausible useful notes. The seven mechanical gains are
not seven useful recoveries. These few practical errors, rather than a demand
for perfect classification, weaken the case for replacement. Existing retained
notes may also change root placements; the eleven-note review is complete for
unique-note coverage changes, not for all changed root assignments.

### What averaging explains, and what it does not

A post-result ordering diagnostic on the fixed 72-case HR37 native audit finds
seven useful cases dominated in every group by some unwanted case under entry
means, seven under distinct-note means, and eight under closest-two means.
This is a score-order diagnostic, not automatic accuracy, a target of zero
required errors, or permission to fit thresholds from labels.

Concrete examples retain the underlying ambiguity:

- Minecraft's two strongest accepted-note affinities in the Games/Events group
  are Games `0.0626043` and Video games `0.0537476`, averaging `0.0581759`.
  Asynchronous operations instead matches Events `0.0594410` and Games
  `0.0569908`, averaging `0.0582159`. The group's closest-two threshold is
  `0.0668853`, set by an Events member. Both are rejected. Lowering this group's
  boundary cannot admit Minecraft while excluding the slightly higher unwanted
  score. This is not solely an excessively strict final coverage boundary.
- YouTube's two closest matches are Image sharing and Photography, averaging
  `0.0509212`. Digestion matches Creativity and Art, averaging `0.0605142`.
  Both miss the group's coverage threshold `0.0849567`. Closest-two support
  still fails to put the useful candidate ahead of this unwanted topic.

Whole-group dilution therefore explains some useful recoveries, but replacing
all-member means with the two nearest notes does not generally resolve the
semantic score confusions. Do not keep sweeping neighbor counts until a small
panel looks perfect. The useful next evaluation is practical whole-note quality
of the existing full-meaning candidate across roots, rather than another
ordering-only claim or a new admission constant.

### Evidence and checks

Ignored directory: `target/food-halves-v1/note-aggregation-admission-v1/`.
Frozen plan SHA256:
`08eff31c72067226a572c207c73b95cd867bd016bbf2ba37da3850ea34ec51a6`.
It pins 60 source/data files before new aggregation scores or admissions; all
remain unchanged after the run. Retained artifacts include all six methods'
query/root outcomes, all new query/group scores, member boundaries and reference
note routes, frozen panel results, four human controls, ordering diagnostics,
nearest-note traces, complete unique-note changes and post-result assessments.
Both original corpus collections used in the new review verify ASv6.100 provenance.

The matched calculation uses 14 reference collections and 327 fixed groups,
checks 7,692 member scores and 1,488,681 query/group cells, replays old decisions
exactly, and verifies query isolation. Maximum scalar score discrepancy is
`2.22e-16`; reference and query mean replays differ by at most `6.66e-16` and
`2.78e-16`. Of 327 groups, 303 use two-note support and 24 use the explicit
one-other-note setting; none is unavailable here. Each coverage result is a
subset of its own score's weakest-member proposals. Eligible accepted-reference
notes retain their routes; actual memberships never change. The numerical
calculation takes 11.69 seconds after input verification; disk reads and hashing
are additional. Relevant regression suites pass 81 tests. No new encodings,
LLM calls, training, paid resources or hierarchy/UI changes occur.

## Whole-note audit of the unchanged full-meaning candidate

See [Whole-note admission audit v1](hierarchy-whole-note-admission-audit-v1.md)
for the frozen 67-note review across HR36/HR37. Primary hash-selected proposals
have useful routes for 19/24 and 18/24 notes, respectively; selected-root reviews
also expose 5/38 and 22/72 unsupported placements, with ambiguity kept separate.
This is genuine coverage value with substantial browsing noise, not a demand
for perfection or a production approval. No scores, prompts, encoder weights,
boundaries, hierarchy membership or prior exact-entry judgments changed.

## Accepted-reference mean-centering follow-up

The [mean-centering v1 matched trial](hierarchy-reference-centering-v1.md)
changes only the saved full-meaning score and consistently recalculates the
existing boundaries. It preserves all 37 originally useful primary-sample
notes and adds two useful HR37 notes, but unsupported root placements change
from 5 to 10 in HR36 and 22 to 19 in HR37. It is a tradeoff, not a consistent
improvement or an activated admission rule. Groups, encoder and hierarchies
remain unchanged; no LLM calls, encodings or training occur.

## Admission-boundary provenance and reference-note bend follow-up

The [provenance audit](hierarchy-admission-boundary-provenance-v1.md) traces weak
accepted members and exact score-order conflicts behind the wrong placements.
The [reference-note bend test](hierarchy-note-bend-admission-v1.md) then replaces
the weakest-note multiplier with a reference-only bend while keeping existing
memberships. It reduces wrong primary-sample root placements from 27 to 1 under
original scores, but useful omitted notes fall from 37 to 14 and two human Food
acceptances are omitted. Of its 16 proposed primary-sample notes, 14 are useful,
one wrong and one unresolved. The user clarified that it should remain a
conservative candidate, with lower coverage as a tradeoff. The unchanged-rule
fresh-data validation is reported below. It is not activated
in HiGen. No hierarchy, encoder, group membership or original score changes
occur.

## HR38 fresh-data transfer of the conservative candidate

The [frozen HR38 transfer](hierarchy-hr38-conservative-admission-v1.md) tests
the unchanged reference-note bend on a 2,000-note corpus, excluding prior note
IDs and exact post-text overlaps. It scores 499 fresh omitted notes with usable
meanings. Candidate proposals cover 384 notes, versus 492 for the broader
baseline; none are applied to the hierarchy.

On 32 hash-selected candidate proposals, 27 have a supported selected route,
four have only wrong routes and one is unresolved. Their 56 root placements
include 33 supported, 15 wrong and eight unresolved. Eleven wrong placements
pass one People group: title-vector pair cosine has median 0.9873, versus
0.2839 for full-meaning vectors despite consistent human-being definitions.
Optional examples are a representation hypothesis, not a proven sole cause.

In separate omission screens, six of 12 baseline-only notes and four of seven
neither-policy notes have a clear missed useful route. These are coverage
costs, not new rejections. All new reviews remain provisional agent assessments.
The candidate retains useful conservative value, while uniform low-error
generalization is unproven. Ninety numerical tests and frozen-input, native
vector-replay, scalar-score and all-301-threshold checks pass. No LLM calls,
training, paid resources, hierarchy/prompt changes or UI update occurred.

## Controlled optional-example pooling diagnosis

The [three-view HR38 probe](hierarchy-meaning-example-probe-v1.md) keeps the
People group and outsiders fixed. Selecting only the definition prefix raises
median within-group cosine from 0.2839 to 0.9480 while examples remain in
context; removing them from context raises it to 0.9505. Pooling optional example
words therefore explains most of this group's weak agreement.

Neither reference-only prefix condition establishes an admission improvement.
With the same rule consistently recalculated, both admit zero of seven useful
controls and zero of 11 reviewed wrong cases. Holding original thresholds fixed
leaves two useful and nine wrong cases admitted. The score ordering of six
actual human classes against those wrong activities also worsens. No cutoff
is tuned, existing membership is preserved and the original conservative
candidate remains unchanged. All 102 relevant tests and independent numerical
checks pass. The proposed next check uses already-saved contextual-title scores
on the same cases; it has not been run. No LLM calls or training occurred.
